ABOUT THE COVER
In 2015, The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. added to the numerous milestones it accomplished in the last few years
within the framework of its strategic plan. The migration to a more powerful trading system, finalization of plans to move
to a new headquarters, and championing of corporate governance initiatives are among the highlights in the past year that
contribute to laying the foundation that will support the achievement of bigger milestones in the years to come.
===== page 4 / 152 =====
9
VISION,
MISSION,
& CORPORATE
VALUES
VISION
A premier exchange with world-class standards for trading securities
and raising capital that serves as a strong engine for a robust economy
MISSION
• Offer products and services responsive to the needs of investors and
other stakeholders
• Provide a facility for fair, accurate, complete and timely information
about listed companies, while extending market education and
awareness programs to investors
• Be a preferred venue for raising capital
• Practice and promote good governance within the Exchange and
among listed companies and trading participants
• Operate efficiently to optimize shareholder value
• Adopt world-class systems and global best practices for an efficient,
fair and orderly market
• Develop a highly motivated and professional workforce, committed to
serve and excel
CORPORATE VALUES
Professionalism in delivering quality service and in meeting the
highest standards of excellence
Integrity, transparency and accountability in implementing business
programs and enforcing decisions
Teamwork in working towards a common and favorable goal for the
market
Mutual respect in relating with fellow employees
Inner strength in prioritizing the common good of the market instead
of individual interest
Corporate responsibility in promoting market growth hand in hand
with community welfare

CHAIRMAN'S MESSAGE
Dear Shareholders,
Our goal of providing more services and products to the market
continued to be the overarching direction that your Board and
Management took in 2015. In order to achieve this target, we needed
to ensure that major milestones were completed while at the same
time, being mindful that the granular details to get there were also
accomplished.
For this reason, it was important that we continued our focus on our vision
however the economic backdrop presented itself. The first part of the year
started with great promise as the PSEi, the country's main market index,
began trekking new record highs, breaching the 8,000-point level in April.
The tide started to turn, however, in the middle of the year, as US
policymakers made clearer indications of a Fed interest rate hike. Later in
the year, the Chinese currency started to depreciate, introducing
uncertainties on how markets will cope with this new global schematic,
which only further compounded the volatilities.
The strong market activity during the first half of the year did provide a
solid cushion for the market when the trends became rather unfavorable.
For the entire year, market liquidity remained intact from the previous year
at P8.96 billion average daily value turnover, closing slightly higher by two
percent from 2014. This resulted in our trading-related revenues to be
relatively stable amidst the challenging environment.
Unfortunately, listing activities were not spared from the global downturn.
As we saw 63 percent of blue chip indices globally closing lower in 2015,
including our own PSEi, companies reassessed their funding plans
through the stock exchange, which affected our listing-related revenues for
the year.

CHAIRMAN'S MESSAGE

"2016 presents new
opportunities agam for the
Exchange. With the power
of new technologies put in
place, we look forward to
another landmark year as
we schedule thé roll-obt of
new products and services
for the tparket. '
Given the market volatilities, it became even more necessary that the
projects we aimed to accomplish for the year were completed with
certainty.
In 2015, we are happy to report that we were able to accomplish major
milestones to support and enhance our capability to reach our goals. With
the collaboration and support from all stakeholders, the Exchange was
able to successfully implement its new trading engine, PSEtrade XTS
powered by NASDAQ within 11 months. The PSEtrade XTS is built with
three times more capacity than the previous trading engine and allows for
a faster recovery in the event of any system disruptions. Together with this,
the new system we installed is capable of expanding to process more
product lines consistent with our plans to grow our offerings beyond the
cash market.
Another important milestone is the finalization of the move to the unified
office at the Fort Bonifacio Global City that will integrate the two existing
trading floors located in Makati and in Ortigas. Once completed, the unified
office is aimed at introducing efficiencies and opening opportunities for the
expansion of the company.
Our governance initiatives have also started to gain more traction. The
Philippines was the proud host of the first ASEAN Corporate Governance
Congress held in November which saw the PSE garner the second highest
number of listed companies among the top 50 companies in ASEAN for
corporate governance, besting the stock markets in Indonesia, Malaysia,
Singapore, and Vietnam.
To cap all these achievements, your Philippine Stock Exchange was once
again recognized as the Best Stock Exchange in Southeast Asia by Alpha
Southeast Asia. The award cited the continued interest of investors in the
Philippine market as well as the governance initiatives that have been
undertaken by the Exchange. This is the second time such recognition has
been given to the PSE in the past three years.
We have also set out our plan to acquire majority of the Philippine
Dealings System, Inc. This move would have facilitated and hastened the
growth of the country's capital market by combining the expertise at both
Exchanges, minimizing systemic risks through vertical integration and
introducing efficiencies that would have redounded to lower friction costs.
Unfortunately, our regulator had a different view about this landmark
transaction. Despite this, we would like to assure you, our shareholders,
that our goal of furthering the expansion of our market remains
unperturbed.
2016 presents new opportunities again for the Exchange. With the power of
new technologies put in place, we look forward to another landmark year
as we schedule the roll-out of new products and services for the market.
Let me personally express my deepest gratitude to all stakeholders for
supporting our initiatives and working tirelessly, together with the Board,
Management and Staff of the PSE, for us to keep reaching new
milestones for the company, the market and the country.
Mabuhay!
Jose T. Pardo
Chairman

PRESIDENT'S MESSAGE
Dear Fellow Shareholders,
What we set out in 2015 was focused on setting the stock market
landscape to enable the Philippine Stock Exchange fulfill its commitment
of offering more products and services. As we closed the final year of the
Company's 2013-2015 strategic plan, we can look back at 2015 with a
great sense of fulfillment for all the feats we completed and the clear
vision of future milestones we can establish.
Bold Moves
Amidst the market volatilities that we faced in 2015, we knew we had to stay
determined to face the work at hand. We were able to complete the migration
and implementation of a new trading system in less than a year. Such a
complicated and massive task would normally take a long time to complete but
we did it in less than a year. With this feat, we became part of the growing
number of exchanges that run the widely used NASDAQ X-stream Trading
System. The technology upgrade allows the Exchange to handle higher trading
volumes. We saw the average number of trades go up by 39.0 percent from a
little over 38,000 per day in 2014 to 53,000 trades per day in 2015.
The new system was also equipped with more sophisticated risk management
features such as provisions for same-day recovery. To complement this effort,
the Exchange requested all trading participants to be connected to the disaster
recovery site and conducted various testing of this new feature throughout the
year.
As we laid the groundwork and finished the technological infrastructure for
trading, we took the first step towards building our new headquarters. This new
property development will unify our Ayala and Tektite trading floors with the
finalization of the agreement with Ayala Land, Inc. and Fort Bonifacio
Development Corporation. The new building will be located at the heart of the
Fort Bonifacio Global City and is expected to be turned over in 2016.
04
Wider Investor Base
Our market education initiatives focused on utilizing web technology to reach
out to more investors across the Philippines and to our overseas Filipinos.
We conducted more stock market webinars tackling basic stock market topics,
fundamental and technical analysis as well as exchange traded funds. The
online seminars were well-attended by our "kababayans" who are based
overseas and are looking for investment opportunities here in the country.
We also engaged more brokers to sign up with our PSEtradex platform, a
system that enables them to offer online trading for their clients.
These initiatives helped sustain the growth in the number of online investors in
the Philippine market. For 2015, total online accounts reached nearly 240,000,
a 35 percent jump from the 175,000 online accounts recorded in 2014.
Good Corporate Governance
Business continuity programs and initiatives remain top of mind at the
Exchange particularly with the system changes that were undertaken in the
past year. For the new trading system project, the Exchange conducted
numerous disaster recovery tests to minimize or mitigate technology
interruptions around the network and infrastructure. The company started its
records digitization under a comprehensive records management framework
that was rolled out during the year. The digital records are properly stored both
onsite and offsite consistent with business continuity processes and objectives.
Our advocacy of promoting good governance continued in close
coordination with the Securities and Exchange Commission. As a result of
this collaboration, the Philippine Corporate Governance Blueprint was
launched in November during the ASEAN Corporate Governance
Congress held in Manila.

PRESIDENT'S MESSAGE
MESSAGE

FROM THE

PRESIDENT
capital market. "
-2
a
The PSE recognizes that helping build better communities outside of its
direct constituency is a crucial part of its goal of becoming a better
corporate citizen. Together with Philam Foundation, Inc., the PSE helped
put up school buildings in two locations in Leyte, to help alleviate the
disaster that was left in the wake of Typhoon Haiyan. These school
buildings were completed and formally turned over in 2015.
Financial Performance
Given the significant investment made for these projects, management
ensured that expenses remained in check. For this reason, operating
expenses only slightly increased by 2 percent for the entire year from
P592.16 million in 2014 to P601.91 million in 2015.
The Exchange remained a preferred capital raising venue for companies. A
total of P 184.60 billion was raised through the market, the second highest
amount raised in the history of the PSE. Despite this, listing-related income
went down by 41 percent year-on-year to P483.43 million. The decline was
mostly due to the absence of the large merger & acquisition deals and
initial public offerings compared with the previous year. Some companies
chose to defer their market debut due to uncertainties in the global market.
With the slight uptick in average daily turnover, trading-related income
increased by 4 percent to P309.87 million from P299.26 million the
previous year. This, together with the rise in other income, helped cushion
the impact of lower listing revenues.
In all, total revenues declined by 20 percent from P 1.52 billion to P 1.21
billion in 2015 resulting to a net income decrease of 21 percent to P682.81
million.
Despite this, the Company retained its dividend payout ratio of 82 percent
as your Board approved the declaration of cash dividends to all
shareholders of P7.OO per share by way of regular dividends amounting to
P4.29 per share and special dividends of P2.71 per share.
Inspired Outlook
All our efforts were validated by third party organizations which conferred
awards to the PSE. We were the first recipient of the Stock Exchange
Innovation Award given in Florida, USA by the International Stock
Exchange Executives Emeriti Lennar International Award. We likewise
bagged the Best Stock Exchange in Southeast Asia Award for 2015 from
institutional investment magazine Alpha Southeast Asia. Their
acknowledgement of our work will be an inspiration for us to do even better
to further develop the local capital market.
The coming year may prove to be another challenging and volatile year
given uncertainties stemming from global economic and geopolitical
developments as well as our upcoming national election. However, we
remain confident that our stable economic standing will continue to
support the growth of our capital market.
For this reason, we believe we can deliver more products and services in
the years ahead. The groundwork we established will definitely help
facilitate these new milestones.
We commend our committed and hardworking management team and
employees for ensuring that we are on track to accomplish the goals we
set.
We are truly grateful for the encouragement and guidance provided by our
Board and we thank all of you, our shareholders, for your continued faith in
and support to the Exchange.
Hans B. Sicat
President and CEO

ABOUT THE PSE
ABOUT
THE
PSE
ORTIGAS
06
The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc.
(PSE) is one of the fastest growing
markets in the region. Among the
pioneer exchanges in Asia, it traces
its origins from the country's two
former stock exchanges, the Manila
Stock Exchange (MSE), which was
established in 1927, and the Makati
Stock Exchange (MkSE), which was
established in 1963.
===== page 11 / 152 =====
ABOUT THE PSE
TRADING SCHEDULE
Although both the MSE and the MkSE traded the same stocks of the
same companies, the bourses were separate stock exchanges for nearly
30 years until December 23, 1992, when both exchanges were unified to
become the present-day Philippine Stock Exchange.
In June 1998, the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) granted
the PSE a "Self-Regulatory Organization" (SRO) status, which meant
that the bourse can implement its own rules and establish penalties on
erring trading participants and listed companies.
In 2001 , one year after the enactment of the Securities Regulation
Code, the PSE was transformed from a non-profit, non-stock,
member-governed organization into a shareholder-based,
revenue-earning corporation headed by a president and a board of
directors. The PSE eventually listed its own shares on the exchange
(traded under the ticker symbol PSE) by way of introduction on
December 15, 2003.
Currently, the PSE maintains two trading floors, one at the PSE Centre
(Tektite), Ortigas Center in Pasig City, and one at its principal office at
the Ayala Tower One & Exchange Plaza in Makati City's Central
Business District.
As of end December 2015, securities traded at the PSE include shares
of 265 listed companies, exchange traded funds and warrants.

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE AT THE PSE
CORPORATE
GOVERNANCE
AT THE PSE
The PSE recognizes the significant role of corporate governance in the overall growth of the
market place. As such, the Exchange advocates the adoption of excellent corporate
governance practices, including the adherence to corporate governance tenets such as
fairness, transparency and accountability, among listed companies and trading participants.
Being at the center of corporate activity among publicly-listed companies, it is incumbent upon
the PSE to lead the promotion of good corporate governance practices not just through
programs and initiatives for listed companies, but by practicing the very principles of corporate
governance that it advocates. This starts by embedding the core principles of good governance
in PSE's mission from which upholding shareholder rights; and timely, fair, and accurate
disclosure emanate. Through the years, living out this particular mission has helped the
Company incorporate good corporate governance practices in its culture.

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE AT THE PSE
THE PHILIPPINE STOCK EXCHANGE, INC.
BOARD OF DIRECTORS
JOSE T. PARDO
CHAIRMAN AND INDEPENDENT DIRECTOR
Mr. Pardo became an Independent Director of the PSE on January 26, 2011 ,
and has been its Chairman since May 2011. He is the Chairman of the
Corporate Governance Committee of the Exchange. He is also the
Chairman of the Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines. He is
Independent Director and Chairman of the Philippine Savings Bank* , Bank
of Commerce, and Philippine Seven Corporation* , and Independent Director
of JG Summit Holdings, lnc*. He is likewise a Director of the National Grid
Corporation of the Philippines, ZNN Radio Veritas, Araneta Hotels, Inc. and
Monte Oro Grid Resources Corporation. Mr. Pardo held various positions in
government including Secretary of the Department of Finance and
Secretary of the Department of Trade and Industry. Mr. Pardo obtained his
Bachelor of Science in Commerce and his Master's Degree in Business
Administration at the De La Salle University in Manila, which he now heads
as Chairman of the Board of Trustees.
HANS B. SICAT
PRESIDENT AND CHIEF EXECUTIVE OFFICER, DIRECTOR
Mr. Sicat has been a PSE Director since May 2009. He has been the
President and Chief Executive Officer of the PSE since January 2011. He
also served as Chairman and Independent Director of the PSE from May
2009 to January 2011. He is concurrently President and CEO of the
Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines, Independent Director
of Serica Balanced Fund and Master Fund, Member of Euromoney
Institutional Investor Asia Advisory Board; Independent Director of the
Philippine Dealing System Holdings Corporation; and Board of Trustee of
Securities Investors Protection Fund; Director of Sky Cable Corporation,
among others. In 2015, he was appointed as Chair of the Emerging
Market Working Group of the World Federation of Exchanges. He has
more than two decades of experience as an investment banker. Mr. Sicat
finished his coursework for Ph.D. Economics Program at the University of
Pennsylvania, Philadelphia, and earned his Master of Arts in Economics
and Bachelor of Science in Mathematics at the University of the
Philippines. He was conferred an Honorary Doctorate Degree in
Business Administration by the Western University in Thailand.
EMMANUEL O. BAUTISTA
DIRECTOR
Mr. Bautista has been a Director of PSE since May 2011 and a member of
the Exchange's Risk Management Committee. He also serves as Director of
the Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines and the Philippine
Depository and Trust Corporation, and is an independent Director of AlG
Philippines Insurance Inc. He is currently the Chairman and Chief Executive
Officer of Deutsche Regis Partners Inc., an affiliate of Deutsche Bank AG,
where he has worked since 1994. From 1986 to 1994, he was connected
with Pepsi Co., Inc. both in New York and in Manila. He was Director of
Strategic Planning and member of the Executive Committee of PepsiCo
Philippines. Mr. Bautista trained as an analyst at Philippine American
Investment Corporation and Bank of America in Los Angeles. He graduated
from the Ateneo de Manila University with a Bachelor of Science degree in
Management Engineering. He holds a Master's degree in Business
Administration from Georgetown University, USA.

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE AT THE PSE
ANABELLE L. CHUA
DIRECTOR
Ms. Chua first became a Director of PSE in 2004 until May 2010 and
was re-elected in May 2011 and is a member of the Exchange's Audit
Committee. She has been the Chief Financial Officer of the Philippine
Long Distance Telephone Company* (PLDT) Group since May 2015,
and was previously the Chief Financial Officer of Smart
Communications, Inc., and Treasurer of PLDT. Her prior professional
experience includes holding various positions at Citibank N.A. and the
Solid Pacific Finance Ltd. in Hong Kong. Ms. Chua graduated Magna
Cum Laude from the University of the Philippines with a degree in
Business Administration and Accountancy and was a consistent
University Scholar for ten semesters. She topped the Certified Public
Accountant Licensure Examination in October 1982.
FRANCIS C. CHUA
DIRECTOR
Ambassador Chua has been a Director of PSE since 2010. He is the Founding
Chairman of BA Securities and the Chairman Emeritus of the Philippine
Chamber of Commerce and Industry Inc. (PCCI) as well as the Honorary
President of the Federation of Filipino-Chinese Chambers of Commerce and
Industry Inc. He is currently the President of the Foundation for Crime
Prevention and the President of the Philippine Business Center. He is also the
Founding Chairman of International Chamber of Commerce, Philippines. He
served as President of three of the most influential business organizations in the
country - PCCI, The Chamber of Commerce of the Philippines Foundation Inc.,
and the Federation of the Filipino Chinese Chambers of Commerce and
Industry. Amb. Chua is also the Consul General (ad honorem) of the Honorary
Consulate General of Peru in Manila since 2006. He is the Chairman of the
Board of Negros Navigation, 2GO Group, Inc.*, DongFeng Automotive
Philippines, and the Green Army Foundation. He is Vice Chairman and Excom
Chair of Bank of Commerce and Vice Chairman of Basic Energy Corporation*.
He has served as Director of various companies and academic institutions and
has been conferred several awards by different organizations.
DAVID O. CHUA
DIRECTOR
Mr. Chua has been a Director of PSE since 2007. He is currently the
Nominee of Asia Pacific Capital Equities and Securities Corporation and
is a member of the Advisory Board of the Metropolitan Bank and Trust
Company*. He is a Director of Crown Equities Inc.* , Nihao Mineral
Resources International, Inc.*, Dizon Copper — Silver Mines Inc.* , and
Trustees of the University of the East and the University of the East
Ramon Magsaysay Memorial Medical Center. Mr. Chua is also the
President of Cathay Pacific Steel Corporation and the Philippine Steel
Makers Association. He graduated with a BS Financial Services
Management, Honors degree from St. Mary's College of California and
finished his MBA from the Kellogg School of Management of
Northwestern University, Evanston, Illinois and the Hong Kong
University of Science and Technology.

of the Bureau of Internal Revenue, Undersecretary of the Finance

the University of the Philippines. He ranked fifth in the Certified Public
Accountant Licensure Examination in 1968.
Mr. Gobing has been a PSE Director since 2001 and has held various
positions in the Exchange since 1998. He is the Chairman of the
Exchange's Risk Committee. He has also been a Director of Securities
Clearing Corporation of the Philippines since 2006. He is also Treasurer
of Manila Stock Exchange Foundation. He is the President of Lucky
Securities, Inc., and E.G. & Sons Insurance Agency Inc.; Chairman of
Jacqui Foods Corp., G.G. Food Chain Corp. J.G. Food Corp.; and
President of Teishoku Dining Concepts Inc. Mr. Gobing graduated with a
Bachelor of Science degree in Management from the University of Santo
Tomas.

DIRECTOR

RAMON S. MONZON
INDEPENDENT DIRECTOR
Mr. Monzon was elected PSE Director in May 2015 and serves as Chairman of the PSE
Audit Committee and Member of the Risk and Nominations and Elections Committees.
He is also a Director and Member of the Executive, Risk and Audit Committees of
Philippine Dealing System Holdings Corporation, Philippine Dealing & Exchange

Chairman and President of PH GSA, Inc., Xpert Air Services, Inc., Karilagan Int'l Travel &
Tours Corp., and Carousel Productions, Inc. From October 1995 to March 2012, he was
the Vice Chairman and Chief Operating Officer of Citadel Holdings, Inc. and served as
President and CEO of Citadel's subsidiaries which included companies in aviation
services and companies in the airline passenger and cargo general sales agency group.
He was the President and CEO of Pacific Rim Export & Holdings Corp. from 1988 to
1995 and was the EVP and Chief Operating Officer of Radio Philippines Network,
Intercontinental Broadcasting Corp, Banahaw Broadcasting Corp and Sining Makulay,
Inc. from 1984-1986. He was also a Partner of SyCip Gorres Velayo & Company from
1982-1984. Mr. Monzon holds a Master of Business Administration degree from the
University of Chicago, a Bachelor of Arts degree major in Political Science from the
Ateneo de Manila University, and a Bachelor of Science degree major in Accounting from
the Manuel L. Quezon University. He is a Certified Public Accountant and a Fellow of the
Institute of Corporate Directors.

Corporate Governance Committee
The PSE Board is fully committed to aligning the Exchange's corporate
governance practices to internationally accepted standards. As such, it
has designated the Corporate Governance Committee to assist the
Board of Directors on issues directly connected with the Exchange's
internal processes, work procedures, and performance; the performance
of the President and his Management team; compensation, benefits and
incentives; succession planning; overall corporate governance of the
Exchange; and market governance.

BOARD COMMITEES
Nominations and Elections Committee
The Nominations and Elections Committee (NOMELEC) is an
independent committee created following the mandate of the PSE
Manual on Corporate Governance and the Securities Regulation Code
(SRC). In accordance with the SRC, NOMELEC is composed of at least
three members, one of whom must be an independent director. The
Board of Directors has appointed two independent directors to the
present NOMELEC, over and above the minimum requirement provided
by the SRC. The NOMELEC is tasked with formulating, screening,
evaluating and studying qualifications of directors, as well as those
recommended to other positions requiring appointment by the Board of
Directors. It is likewise tasked with completing the list of directors for
nominations in accordance with the SRC as well as finalizing such rules
and regulations it may formulate within its jurisdiction for approval by the
Board.

CORPORATE GOVERNANCE POLICIES
Honest and Ethical Conduct
The PSE expects all employees to act with the highest standards of
honesty and ethical conduct while working on Exchange premises, at
offsite locations where Exchange business is being conducted, at
Exchange-sponsored business and social events, or at any other place
where the employees are representing the Exchange.
Compliance with Laws, Rules and Regulations
As a publicly-listed company, the Exchange is subject to numerous laws
and regulations, including its own Listing and Disclosure Rules. Obeying
the law, both in letter and in spirit, is the foundation on which this
Company's ethical standards are built. All employees and officers must
respect, obey and comply with the laws, rules and regulations
applicable to PSE's business.
Bribes and Kickbacks
Exchange employees are prohibited from offering, giving, soliciting or
accepting bribes or kickbacks. A bribe is defined as a thing of value
given to someone with the intent of obtaining favorable treatment from
the recipient. Kickbacks consist of payment in cash or in kind, including
goods, services, the use of another company's property, or forgiving any
sort of obligation provided to a customer or supplier for the purpose of
improperly obtaining or rewarding favorable treatment in connection with
a transaction. Bribes and kickbacks may not be offered either directly or
through a third party in order to obligate the recipient to return the favor.
Fair and Free Markets
The Exchange is committed to promoting free and competitive markets.
It will not tolerate any attempt by any of its employees or representatives
to manipulate or tamper with the markets or the prices of securities or
other financial instruments. The Exchange's goal is to ensure candor
and honesty in all its dealings, including those with business partners,
regulators, other stakeholders and the public.
Conflict of Interest
A "conflict of interest" exists when a person's private interest interferes or
even appears to interfere in any way with the interests of the Company.
A conflict situation can arise when an employee or officer takes actions
or has interests that may make it difficult to perform his or her Company
work objectively and effectively. Conflicts of interest may also arise when
an employee or officer, or a member of his or her family (up to the 2nd
degree of consanguinity and 1st degree of affinity) receives improper
personal benefits as a result of his or her position in the Company.
The PSE has an obligation to the marketplace and to its shareholders to
make sound business decisions that are not influenced by the personal
interests of its employees. This means that PSE employees are
expected to act in the interest of the Exchange, without favor or
preference based on possible direct or indirect personal gain.
Employees must avoid and, if needed, remove themselves from
relationships and activities that may lead to a conflict of interest or the
appearance of a conflict of interest.
Personal Trading and Insider Information
Employees of the Exchange are prohibited from buying or selling
securities listed on the Exchange, whether for their own account or for
the account of another. An exception is trading PSE shares under an
approved Employee Stock Purchase Plan and subject to the proper
disclosure to the Securities & Exchange Commission. Subscribing to
initial public offerings (IPO) from listed companies or underwriters is
likewise prohibited. These prohibitions, besides being imposed by
Exchange rules, are to avoid actual or potential conflicts of interest, or of
CORPORATE GOVERNANCE AT THE PSE
the perception of conflict of interest, arising from the possession by
Exchange employees of insider information resulting from their work in
the Exchange.
Employees may, at some time, have access to information related to our
company or to other issuers that is not known to the general public. All
non-public information about the Company or other issuers should be
considered confidential information and should not generally be
disclosed to third parties (including family members) or used for any
direct or indirect personal benefit unless or until such information is
disclosed to the public.
Employees and officers who have access to confidential information
about the Company or any other entity are not permitted to use or share
that information for trading purposes in the Company's or the other
entity's securities or for any other purpose except the conduct of the
Company's business. To use non-public information for personal
financial benefit or to "tip" others who might make an investment
decision on the basis of this information is not only unethical but also
illegal.
Gift, Meal and Entertainment Policy
The purpose of gifts and business entertainment in a commercial setting
is to create goodwill and sound working relationships, not to give or gain
unfair advantage to or from third parties. These third parties may include
but not be limited to existing or potential vendors, suppliers, service
providers and business partners, regulators, as well as the Exchange's
regulated entities such as listed companies, trading participants and
listing applicants.
Employees or any of their family members up to the 2nd degree of
consanguinity and 1st degree of affinity have an obligation not to solicit,
accept, offer or provide any entertainment, meals or gifts that are not
appropriate or could be perceived as an improper attempt to influence
business. They may only accept gifts which can be considered token
gifts or corporate giveaways and other related circumstances.
Corporate Opportunities
Employees and officers are prohibited from taking for themselves
opportunities that arise or are discovered in the course of their
employment at the Exchange or through the use of corporate property,
information or position without the consent of the President and CEO.
No employee or officer may use corporate property, information, or
position for personal gain, and no employee or officer may compete with
the Company directly or indirectly. Employees and officers owe a duty to
the Company to advance its legitimate interests when the opportunity to
do so arises.
A non-cash gift given in consideration of an employee's lecture,
speaking engagement, participation in a forum and the like, sponsored
or arranged by an outside organization, shall be governed by the
Exchange's gift policy.
This policy on honoraria and fees does not apply to an employee's
private or personal speaking engagements not endorsed by the
Exchange provided that such activities do not conflict with the interests
of the Exchange.
Outside Employment and Directorships
An employee may not accept outside employment or directly or
indirectly conduct outside business that interferes with the proper
performance of his or her job at the Exchange, is conducted during his
or her normal working hours, utilizes confidential information and other
assets of the Exchange (including proprietary assets) or specialized
skills and knowledge gained as an employee of the Exchange,
competes with the Exchange or otherwise conflicts with the interests of
17
===== page 22 / 152 =====
CORPORATE GOVERNANCE AT THE PSE
the Exchange. This includes becoming a contractor, consultant or
supplier to the Exchange while being employed at the Exchange.
Outside consultancies are prohibited outright.
All officers and employees of the Exchange must disclose any and all
outside employment or business activity to the Employee Services
Department, for which approval by the Corporate Governance
Committee upon endorsement by the President and CEO should be
obtained. Outside employment or business activity may be diSapproved
anytime if it is determined that it would conflict with the interests of the
Exchange.
Related-Party Business Dealings
An employee must disclose to the Corporate Governance Office any
business relationship or proposed business transaction that the
Company may have with any company in which he or she or a related
party (spouse, children, sibling', parents) has a direct or indirect interest
(employment, directorship„controlling ownership) or from which he or a
related party may derive a benefit, if such a relationship or transaction
might give rise to a conflict of interest or the appearance of a conflict of
interest (for examéle, if the employee or a family member owns or
controls propprfy of significant value that the Company is either
purchasing or leasing).
Anti-Fraud Policy
Employees have a responsibility to identify and ensure that situations
involving fraud do not occur. Fraud is the intentional misrepresentation
or concealment of a material fact for the purpose of procuring an unjust
or unlawful benefit. Fraud encompasses all forms of theft, including
intellectual property, identity theft and manipulation or misuse of
Company information or assets. Employees are encouraged to report
suspected fraud through the proper channels. Retaliation for reporting,
in good faith, a violation is prohibited and will itself result in disciplinary
action.
Raising Ethical Issues
If an employee encounters an unethical situation or faces an issue
where he or she has concerns or is not sure what to do, he or she must
think the situation through and ask the following questions until the
employee is confident that he or she is doing the appropriate thing:
What specifically am I being asked to do?
• Is it consistent with our Code, applicable policies and laws?
Does it feel like the right thing to do, or does it look or feel
unethical or improper?
What might be the consequences of my action or inaction?
Further guidance may be sought by the employee from his Department
or Division Head or the Corporate Governance Office.

HANS B. SICAT
President & Chief Executive Officer
Mr. Sicat, 55, was named President and CEO of the Exchange in January
2011. He also served as Chairman and Independent Director of the PSE from
May 2009 to January 2011. He is concurrent President and CEO of the
Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines. Currently, he is
non-executive Vice Chairman and Director of LegisPro Corporation. Mr. Sicat
also holds positions in various business entities such as Independent
Director of Serica Balanced Fund and Master Fund, Member of Euromoney
Institutional Investor Asia Advisory Board; Independent Director of the
Philippine Dealing System Holdings Corporation; and Board of Trustee of
Securities Investors Protection Fund; Director of Sky Cable Corporation,
among others. Mr. Sicat has more than two decades of experience as an
investment banker.
ROEL A. REFRAN
Senior Vice President and Chief Operating Officer
Mr. Refran, 42, a certified public accountant-lawyer, joined the PSE in March
2005 as Vice President and General Counsel of the Exchange. He was later
appointed Compliance Officer of the Exchange and Securities Clearing
Corporation of the Philippines. Atty. Refran earned his degree in Bachelor of
Science in Business Administration and Accountancy, Magna Cum Laude, from
the University of the Philippines in 1995. He obtained his Juris Doctor degree,
Second Honors, from Ateneo de Manila University School of Law in 1999. He
ranked among the top 20 successful examinees in the May 1995 CPA licensure
exams, and placed fourth in the 1999 bar examinations. After being awarded the
prestigious Fulbright scholarship, he earned in 2012 his Master of Business
Administration degree, with specialization in capital markets and finance, from
the Goizueta Business School of Emory University (USA) where he was also a
Donald R. Keough scholar. Atty. Refran serves as Chairman of the Philippine
Chamber of Commerce and Industry (PCCI) Capital Markets Development
Committee.
JINKY A. ALORA
Assistant Vice President and Head of Internal Audit Group
Ms. Alora, 47, has been Head of the Internal Audit Group since May 2012.
Prior to this appointment, she was Head of the Trading Participants Regulation
Department and was designated as the Officer-ln-Charge of the Market
Regulation Division from October 2009 until the creation of CMIC in 2012.
Before she joined the Exchange in 1995, Ms. Alora was the Accounting
Manager of USA Laboratories, and a Senior Auditor at Joaquin Cunanan Price
Waterhouse Coopers. She has been with the Exchange for 20 years already.
She graduated Magna Cum Laude with a Bachelor of Science Degree in
Commerce Major in Accounting from the University of Perpetual Help of
Laguna. Ms. Alora is a Certified Public Accountant and earned her Master's
Degree in Business Administration from the Pamantasan ng Lungsod ng
Maynila.
J. ARGEL G. ASTUDILLO
Vice President for Governance, Risk and Compliance and Head of the
Corporate Governance Office
Mr. Astudillo, 57, is the Compliance Officer of the PSE and its subsidiaries,
the Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines and the Capital
Markets Integrity Corporation. Prior to joining the Exchange in December
2010, Mr. Astudillo was a Risk Management Consultant of the Board of
Trustees of the Government Service Insurance System from August 2008 to
June 2010. He was also a Citibanker for 22 years, holding various senior
roles in internal audit, risk management, financial market operations and
compliance in the Philippines. Mr. Astudillo earned his Bachelor of Science
degree in Business Administration from the University of the Philippines and
his Master of Business Administration from Case Western Reserve University
in the United States.
MARSHA M. RESURRECCION
Assistant Vice President and Head of Issuer Regulation Division
Ms. Resurreccion, 40, started her career with the Exchange as a Listings
Specialist in July 2002. She was promoted to Senior Listings Specialist with
the rank of Supervisor in September 2004. By March 2006, she was made
Assistant Manager and a year later she became a full-fledged Manager. In
July 2008, Ms. Resurreccion was promoted to Senior Manager and Head of
the Listings Department. Prior to joining the Exchange, Ms. Resurreccion
was an Officer Development Program Trainee at Solidbank Corporation
where she also held the position of Regional Marketing Officer for Mindanao
and Account Assistant of its Branch Lending Division. She was also
Marketing Consultant and Compliance Officer/ Branch Cashier at South
Bank, Inc. in Cagayan de Oro City. She has been with the Exchange for 13
years. Ms. Resurreccion has a Bachelor of Science in Business Management
degree from the Ateneo de Manila University, graduating with Honorable
Mention.
ELISA L. BENAVIDEZ
Officer-in-Charge, Controllership and Treasury Division and Concurrent Head of
Budget and Treasury Department
Ms. Benavidez, 54, a certified public accountant, has been with the Exchange
for 13 years. Before joining the PSE in January 2003 as Head of Treasury
Department, she was the Vice President for Resource Management Group and
the Controllership Group of Small Business Guarantee and Finance
Corporation. She also worked at the Guarantee Fund for Small and Medium
Enterprises as Manager of Controllership Department. Ms. Benavidez
graduated from the University of the East with a Bachelor of Science degree in
Business Administration.

===== page 29 / 152 =====
STOCK MARKET PERFORMANCE & HIGHLIGHTS
After an unprecedented run of six years of consecutive annual growth, the Philippine stock market's
momentum was halted by adverse developments in both domestic and international fronts. Despite a
rousing start to the year that saw the benchmark index again rewriting the record books, majority of the
succeeding months saw global markets struggle against reports of lower-than-expected growth,
interest rate hikes, potential debt defaults, and global geopolitical concerns. This shadow of uncertainty
primarily led to declining volumes and massive foreign outflows. But even as the local market took a
step back, there was little doubt on its resilience as strong macro-and-micro-fundamentals still
managed to put the economy in a better position relative to other markets.
The Philippine stock market mirrored the rosy prospects of the economy well into the first four
months of the year, only to lose its momentum later on. The PSEi managed to hit 27 new closing
highs, with the latest record at 8, 127.48 points on April 10, before a confluence of domestic and
foreign issues contributed to the eventual downtrend of the index by yearend. The benchmark index
snapped its six-year winning streak to end 2015 with a loss of 3.9 percent or 278.49 points to
6,952.08 points from 7,230.57 points at the end of 2014. Despite this declining performance, the
PSEi was still the second best performing index against other ASEAN markets, bettered only by
Vietnam's VN Index, which registered a 6.1 percent gain.

===== page 31 / 152 =====
Throughout the year, the local stock market was beset with challenging
external developments which made their way to the headlines,
indicating that difficult times still lay ahead for the global economy.
The Philippine economy, for one, struggled to achieve the high growth
targets it set for the year. The country's performance fell short of
expectations when the first quarter gross domestic product (GDP) only
came in at 5.0 percent, the lowest level since 2011. The growth rates
logged in the succeeding quarters, while slightly better and respectable
than the last, were still deemed insufficient to propel the economy toward
the 7.0 percent to 8.0 percent goal, as the country ended 2015 with a full
year GDP of 5.8 percent. The lower-than-expected economic
performance was attributed to low demand for merchandise exports,
which slid by 5.6 percent to US$58.65 billion for the full year of 2015 from
US$62.10 billion in 2014. The resulting figures prompted multilateral
institutions to trim growth forecasts as the year drew to a close.
In June, Greece was once more at the center of a turmoil following the
election of an anti-austerity party. The Greek government tried
negotiating the bailout terms with its creditors much to the detriment of
the banking system, prompting a bank closure and the implementation
of a cash withdrawal limit for more than a week. With bailout talks at a
deadlock alongside the citizens' resounding NO in a referendum,
Greece became the first developed country to default on its debt
payment to the International Monetary Fund. This spurred fears of a
Greek exit from the Eurozone and also questioned the stability of the
European Union as a bloc even after Greece eventually struck a US$95
billion bailout deal, its third aid in five years.
Meanwhile, China continued to struggle in its attempts to jumpstart a
slowing economy. In August, the People's Bank of China surprisingly
devalued the yuan by 1.9 percent against the US dollar, allowing market
forces to influence the currency's movement. Economists and investors
were, however, divided on the reason behind the move. On one hand,
STOCK MARKET PERFORMANCE & HIGHLIGHTS
some viewed it as an effort to boost falling export numbers, while others
say it was aimed at having the currency admitted into the International
Monetary Fund's basket of reserve currencies as China seeks global
prominence. Amidst all these, China's economic health remained a
cause for worry pending a successful transition from being
investment-led to being consumer-driven.
Market sentiment was similarly roiled by the uncertainty on the US
Federal Reserve's (US Fed) monetary policy actions following the end of
its quantitative easing program in 2014. While investors expected a rise
in US key rates, they were particularly concerned on the pace and
frequency of such rate hikes. The anxiety built up leading to September,
when the US Fed held off any rate hike on the back of weak global
economic conditions. The much-anticipated rate lift-off happened in
December, when the US Fed increased its federal funds rate by 25
basis points for the first time since 2008. This saga alone caused the
Philippines' net foreign portfolio investments to book a net outflow for the
second straight year in the amount of US$599.69 million, 93.3 percent
higher than the US$310.21 million net outflow the year before.
Trades at the oil market were volatile, with prices spiraling down in the
latter part of the year. At the New York Mercantile Exchange (NYMEX),
crude oil prices continued to trade below the US$IOO per barrel level
and only managed to reach a high of US$61.43 per barrel within the first
semester. Soft demand from advanced economies at a time of an oil
supply glut further added to the volatility on NYMEX trades, pulling
down the crude oil price to US$30 per barrel level for the first time. By
yearend, the price of crude oil settled at US$37.04 per barrel, 30.5
percent lower than the US$53.27 per barrel close in 2014.
In the market for precious metals, the price of gold traded between
US$I ,050.80 per ounce and US$I ,300.70 per ounce at the US
Commodity Exchange. Trading of gold was volatile for the most part of
the year before slowly trending lower as the year wrapped up. The price

STOCK MARKET PERFORMANCE & HIGHLIGHTS
of the yellow metal ended at US$I ,060.20 per ounce, down by 10.5
percent from the US$I , 184.10 per ounce year-on-year, as demand
waned on expectations that the US economy is onto a recovery path.
The external environment proved to be a challenge for the earnings
performance of listed companies. For the January to September period,
the consolidated net income of listed companies dropped by 1.8 percent
to P441.40 billion from P449.56 billion in the same period in 2014. This
occurred despite the marginal 0.6 percent decrease in consolidated
revenues to P4.90 trillion from P4.93 trillion in the same period in 2014.
The decline in overall earnings performance of listed companies was
primarily brought about by higher foreign exchange losses as the
Philippine peso depreciated against the US dollar. The Philippine peso
in fact hit a low of P47.44, a level not seen since 2009. The local
currency finished the year at P47.17, 5.7 percent weaker than the
P44.62 close in 2014.
Most of the stock market indicators were not spared from the downbeat
sentiment. Four of the six sector indices recorded negative growth rates
in 2015, with the Mining and Oil index posting the biggest decline at
34.1 percent. This was followed by the Services, Financials, and
Industrial indices, which fell by 28.0 percent, 8.6 percent, and 7.9
percent, respectively. Bucking the trend was the Holding Firms index as
it increased by 4.8 percent, while the Property index rose by 3.7 percent.
Meanwhile, the broader All Shares index ended the year down by 6.4
percent to 3,990.47 points from 4,264.61 points in 2014.
The market value of shares listed in the PSE retreated from their
year-ago record levels. Total market capitalization dropped by 5.5
percent to P 13.47 trillion from P 14.25 trillion the previous year, while
domestic market capitalization slid by 4.5 percent to P 11.19 trillion from
P 11.71 trillion. The 26.7 percent decrease in the market capitalization of
companies in the Mining and Oil sector primarily pulled down the
domestic market capitalization tally.
Putting these headwinds aside, the country's economy remained well-
positioned in Asia. For one, headline inflation rate in 2015 averaged 1.4
percent, which was significantly lower than the 4.1 percent average a
year earlier. This was likewise well below the government's target band of
2.0 percent and 4.0 percent for the year, allowing the Monetary Board to
keep interest rates steady for 10 straight meetings since its last action in
September 2014. The overnight borrowing and lending rates remained
unchanged at 4.0 percent and 6.0 percent, respectively. Special deposit
account rates and reserve requirement ratios were left untouched as well.
Welcome
Gross international reserves (GIR) in 2015 stood at US$80.61 billion, a
1.3 percent rise from US$79.54 billion in 2014. The 10.3-month import
cover provided by the GIR could attest to the resilience of the Philippine
economy in the face of a fragile external environment. Foreign currency
likewise persistently flowed into the country through cash remittances
from Filipinos working abroad, which continued to be among the main
anchors of the economy as it posted a record US$25.77 billion,
surpassing the previous year's US$24.63 billion.
Credit rating agencies also affirmed the country's investment grade
status amidst the various challenges. Standard & Poor's and Moody's
Investors Service kept their stable outlook on the Philippines' credit
rating, while Fitch Ratings upgraded its outlook from stable to positive. In
addition, Japan Credit Rating Agency, Ltd. raised the Philippines' credit
rating from BBB- to BBB+ with a stable outlook.
However, the Philippines' performance in terms of competitiveness had
mixed results. In the World Economic Forum's 2015-2016 Global
Competitiveness Report, the country scored a five-notch climb to 47th
place from its past ranking after improvement in 12 pillars were noted.
On the other hand, the World Bank's Ease of Doing Business Report
placed the country at 103rd, down from the previous rank of 97th.
At the stock market, total value turnover inched up by 1.0 percent to
P2.15 trillion from P2.13 trillion in 2014, a feat achieved despite foreign
transactions netting a sell of P59.76 billion. Average daily value turnover
likewise grew by 1.8 percent to P8.96 billion from P8.80 billion. Foreign
transactions accounted for 47.9 percent of the total trading activity in the
stock market.
Capital raising activities likewise gained momentum as 29 companies
tapped the local market for their funding needs. Total capital raised in
2015 surged by 20.6 percent to P 184.60 billion from P 153.08 billion the
previous year. This was the second highest capital raised in the
Exchange's history since the record P219.07 billion in 2012. Private
placement transactions cornered the lion's share of capital raised at
P83.90 billion, or 45.4 percent of the pie. Stock rights and follow-on
offerings, meanwhile, contributed P48.40 billion and P47.10 billion,
respectively. The initial public offering (IPO) of four companies, on the
other hand, resulted in P5.20 billion in capital raised. For the second
straight year, the Exchange welcomed the IPO of another company,
Italpinas Development Corporation, under the PSE's enhanced rules for
the Small, Medium and Emerging Board.

STRATEGIC PLAN
STRATEGIC
PLAN
The year 2015 marked the third and final year of the
Exchange's strategic plan which focused on launching
new products and services. The initiatives in the
strategic plan were anchored on three key pillars -
technical expertise, market framework, and technology
structure. Guided by this strategy, the Exchange was
successful in launching new products such as Exchange
Traded Fund, Shari'ah compliance screening of listed
companies, and index futures listed in Singapore
Exchange. Technology infrastructure was also
strengthened through the deployment of new technology
systems which include the PSE EDGE, PSETradex, and
mostly recently, the PSEtrade XTS trading engine.
Looking ahead, the Exchange shall continue to embark
on a new medium-term plan, picking off from the
success and impact on launching new products, while
undertaking initiatives to make the market's growth more
inclusive to allow more Filipinos to participate in wealth
creation. Capacity and technology will also be shaped to
become more responsive to the requirements of the
market for speed and efficiency. These strategic themes
are integral in driving the PSE closer towards its vision
of becoming a world class exchange.
9.
37
===== page 42 / 152 =====
MODERN INFRASTRUCTURE
MODERN
INFRASTRUCTURE
The year 2015 saw the PSE embark on main projects aimed at upgrading its
infrastructure — from technology to its physical structure — to take it a step
closer to realizing its vision of being a premiere exchange for trading securities.
38
===== page 43 / 152 =====
A New Trading System in Place
On June 22, 2015, the PSE migrated to its new trading system, the
PSEtrade XTS using NASDAQ's X-stream Trading technology. The
adoption of the new system was completed after only eleven months of
preparation, much faster than the two years it took for the Exchange to
migrate to the former trading platform.
The PSEtrade XTS, which replaced the NSC trading platform provided
by NYSE Euronext Technologies SAS, is equipped to handle large
trading volumes. It is also capable of supporting the future requirements
of the PSE, including providing more products and services. The PSE is
set to use the PSEtrade XTS for many years as the system can also
accommodate other investments instruments such as fixed income and
derivatives. Furthermore, the system has improved risk management
features such as the capability to recover from a system failure within
the same day, compared to the previous trading platform which can only
recover the next trading day.
2510
200
MODERN INFRASTRUCTURE
NASDAQ's Exchange technology operates in over a hundred
marketplaces across USA, Europe, Asia, Australia, Africa and Middle
East. ASEAN Exchanges that use the platform include Bursa Malaysia,
the Singapore Exchange, and the Indonesia Exchange.
To ensure preparedness of brokers and market participants in the
transition to the new system, the PSE conducted a series of technical
trainings and mandatory market rehearsals. Initial preparations for the
XTS adoption started in 2014, but bulk of the work happened in 2015. To
make sure that they are ready to use the XTS compatible terminals,
traders underwent trainings and certification examination in January 2015.
From March to May 2015, the Exchange held weekend market
rehearsals. Traders, back office personnel of stockbrokerage firms, data
vendors, and various divisions of the Exchange went through whole day
simulated trading sessions using the PSEtrade XTS. The sessions
pspc
PXP
ncg
ruc

MODERN INFRASTRUCTURE
aimed to replicate the behavior of the market under a regular trading
environment with Trading Participants (TPs) entering mock orders which
resulted into trades. Aside from observing the normal trading phase,
from Pre-Open to Trading-at-Last, the market rehearsals tested the
disaster recovery (DR) site of the new system.
To update brokers on the milestones and developments about the
PSEtrade XTS, the PSE initiated weekly forums that also served as an
avenue for all market participants to air their concerns about the new
system. Moreover, a PSEtrade XTS microsite was developed to serve
as a repository of pertinent information on the new system.
Announcements and event notices were also provided through the
dedicated section on the PSE website.
The Exchange likewise formed the PSE Helpdesk consisting of PSE
personnel, to address all PSEtrade XTS and trading-related concerns. It
served as the front line service of the technical team that attended to all
queries and concerns raised by TPs through the PSE hotline, fax or
email.
In line with the launch of the new trading system, data vendors and TPs
were asked to participate in various market rehearsals to test the new
market data feed protocol called ITCH. The ITCH protocol is an efficient
way of distributing market data in terms of bandwidth requirement as it
carries low latency messages. More than half of the licensed data
vendors upgraded their subscriptions to ITCH Total View, the premier
data feed that shows every single quote and order at every price level.
The PSE also launched its latest real-time product called ITCH News,
which enables subscribers to receive company disclosures and
exchange notices in text format and the ETF iNAV, which is released
every minute. In addition, the PSE began offering bundled subscriptions
to clients subscribing to both ITCH News and the Corporate
Announcements Feed or to both ITCH Total View and ITCH Basic,
which displays best bid offer prices only.
In line with the successful launch of PSEtrade XTS, around 15 Front
End Order Management System (FEOMS) were successfully system-
certified and 33 TPs launched their own FEOMS. Another five TPs also
launched their Direct Market Access (DMA) facility. With the DMA facility,
TPs' customers may directly post orders to PSE without the trader's
manual intervention.
A New Headquarters in Progress
Since the PSE was established in 1992, it has had two trading floors —
one in Ayala and one in Ortigas. This setup has remained even after the
unification of the two predecessor Exchanges. Given the growing
requirements of the market and the need to increase efficiencies both for
office operations, system controls and risk management, the need to
unify the two offices has become more and more relevant.
This aspiration will be realized with the acquisition by the PSEofoffice
units in a new building that will rise in Fort Bonifacio GlobaVCity (BGC).
The building will be located at One Bonifacio High Street, which is at th
corner of 5th Avenue and 28th Street in BGC.

Process Automation
Infits mission to optimize shareholder value, the PSE made use of
technology to improve its operational efficiency by enabling a higher
fevel of information transparency in business processes.
PSE Office-Wide Digitization Project
In 2015, the Company continued to digitize itsoffice records in line with
business continuity requirements and information security protocols.
More than 700,000 pages of records frorfl various departments were
scanned, indexed, and stored in a Document Management System that
serves as the repository of the Exchange's digital records. Licenses
were issued to departments ahd offices to facilitate access to the
records, while user-trainings were conducted to familiarize employees in
using the system.
The digitization project began in 2011 with the scanning of the PSE
Library's historical records. These records have been accessible to
library visitors since 2012 through the Electronic Search and Access
Facility of the library.
The digital conversion of an additional 320,000 pages of records is
expected to be completed by the first half of 2016.
Activities Centralized Supervision System (ACCESS PSE)
In line with initiatives to improve operational efficiency and to allow
business units to effectively monitor their projects, an automated project
management system was developed and rolled out to the Exchange's
various departments in 2015. ACCESS PSE, a cloud-based application,
was designed to allow users to immediately effect updates to an activity
and simultaneously enable Management to access and view these
through innovative dashboards, customizable reports, and real-time
notifications.
Design and construction of the automated project management system
began in January 2015, and following the completion of data validation
and migration, ACCESS PSE officially went live on July 16, 2015. The
ACCESS PSE application also included a mobile app, which allows
users to instantly get the latest information on projects and activities on
their smartphones.
MODERN INFRASTRUCTURE
Online Systems for Human Resources and Procurement Services
To increase operational efficiency and promote environment-friendly
practices, the PSE adopted paperless processing of documents utilized
by the Human Resources and Administration Division (HRAD). In the
last quarter of 2015, the Employee Services Department rolled out its
Human Resources Information System (HRIS) / Payroll System in order
to improve its administration of employee information and payroll.
Through the HRIS, monitoring of time sheets and pay slips as well as
filing of leaves, business trips and overtime applications can be done
online. Employee profile and announcements from the HRAD can also
be viewed through the new system.
In June, the Materials Management and Procurement Services
Department also started using the new Purchase Order Module System
designed to speed up the purchase order management workflow of the
Exchange.
With these new systems, the use and printing of forms are no longer
needed, thereby allowing the PSE to save on paper and printing costs.

BIGGER MARKET
BIGGER
MARKET
Leveraging on the growing economy and the favorable
shift in the wealth demographics of the country, the PSE
carried out initiatives and built partnerships to promote
the market to more investors and potential issuers and
make it even more competitive in the region.
Stock Market Investor Profile
The Stock Market Investor Profile survey is conducted by the
Exchange at the end of the year to present a detailed
snapshot of the demographics of the investing public as well
as to aid the Exchange in tracking the expansion of its
investor base. The 2014 results of the Stock Market Investor
Profile was published in mid-2015.
Data on online trading accounts continued to provide stellar
results. In 2014, there were a total of 174,592 online accounts,
an increase of 35.1 percent from the previous year's total of
129,255 online accounts. Since 2010, the total number of
accounts has grown by a compounded annual growth rate
(CAGR) of 48.9 percent.
43
===== page 48 / 152 =====
BIGGER MARKET
To respond to the growing number of online investors, the PSE
developed its own online trading platform called PSETradex. Through
PSETradex, trading participants can offer online trading services to their
clients without having to develop their own system. Since the service
was made available, nine trading participants (TP) already launched
their online trading platforms powered by PSETradex, including Lucky
Securities whose online facility went live in September.
The number of retail accounts per location also posted double-digit
CAGR from 2010 to 2014. Retail accounts in the Visayas continued to
grow by a CAGR of 29.6 percent while retail accounts in Luzon and
Mindanao grew at a CAGR of 27.7 percent and 27.5 percent,
respectively. Metro Manila, on the other hand, saw its retail accounts
grow by 23.5 percent in the same period.
Based on 2014 numbers, 76.7 percent of domestic retail investors were
based in Metro Manila, a drop from the previous year's share to total
accounts of 79.7 percent. The percentage share of retail investors based
in Luzon increased significantly as they cornered 16.1 percent of retail
accounts from 13.4 percent in 2013. Meanwhile, local retail investors
from the Visayas represented 5.1 percent, while investors in Mindanao
increased their share of total retail accounts to 2.0 percent.
In terms of total investor accounts, there was a 9.4 percent increase in
2014 to 640,665 accounts from 585,562 in 2013. Total investor accounts
grew at a CAGR of 6.5 percent between 2010 and 2014.

Trading Participants Assistance
The PSE recorded and responded to more than 8,000 TP issues and
queries in 2015, a 60 percent increase from the previous year's record.
Service requests of TPs that were received and processed also increased
by 37 percent. Almost 2,200 TPs service requests consisted of CCG/DMA
Testing, DRConnection, iPSE Account, Lift in Value Limit, PSE ID, RSA
Security Token, Trading/Viewing Accounts, TradingNiewing Terminals,
and Trading Floor Access. Service requests for Lift in Value Limit, Trading
Accounts, Trading Floor Access and DR Connection got the most number
of requests.
44
Shari'ah Compliant Securities
In 2015, the PSE continued to release the list of Shari'ah-compliant
securities on a quarterly basis, with the list being published on the
Exchange's website by the 5th trading day of each quarter. The
Exchange updated the list in the months of January, April, July, and
October, with 61 , 60, 53, and 58 securities being tagged as
Shari'ah-compliant, respectively.
Islamic investing has also become a staple of the Exchange's market
education campaign, with the Shari'ah list included aea key topic during
PSE-sponsored seminars, roadshows and product forums. The
Exchange is currently studying the possibility pf faunching a Shari'ah
index to aid investors in tracking the performance of PSE's
Shari'ah-compliant stocks. Fund managers may also utilize such index
in creating Shari'ah-based products.
Collaboration with the Public-Private Partnership (PPP) Center of
the Philippines for listipg of PPP Firms
In recent years, the Philippine government has been actively addressing
its infrastructure needs by forging various PPPs to build infrastructure
projects throughout the country. These partnerships were designed to
encourage the participation of the private sector in building more
infraStructure and other public good requirements that are critical for
gconomic growth and development. Through these, the government
hopes to leverage on the sophisticated expertise, state-of-the-art
equipment, and access to adequate financing of the private sector.
Generally, corporate entities engaged in PPPs fund their projects
through project financing loan instruments, issuance of bonds, or
internally generated capital.
PSE President and CEO Hans B. Sicat with panelists during PPP Forum on
October 9, 2015 at Asian Development Bank
Given the growing demand for up-to-date infrastructure in the past
decade, having alternative funding sources for participating entities to
the PPPs can help facilitate the rollout of PPP projects. To date,
however, there is no existing regulatory framework that allows the public
offering of equities solely to raise funds for PPP Projects. This gap has
encouraged the PSE to explore opening the local capital market for the
entry of PPPs as an equity instrument.
In 2015, the PSE initiated various consultations with the major players in
the local PPP industry to determine the feasibility of an equity offering
and to gather information for the creation of rules and regulations for the
listing of PPP firms. Further, the PSE partnered with the Public-Private
Partnership Center of the Philippines (PPP Center), the Securities and
Exchange Commission (SEC) and the Asian Development Bank (ADB),
to organize a series of roundtable discussions to formally bring together
industry players, various regulatory bodies and foreign representatives
to share their respective views and experiences in relation to this
initiative.
The first roundtable conference entitled "Exploring Opportunities and
Challenges in Securities Issuance" was held on August 20, 2015 at the
ADB. It focused on assessing the appetite of entities engaged in PPP
Projects to raise funding through securities issuance.
===== page 49 / 152 =====
The resource speakers, composed of representatives from the SEC,
PSE and the PPP Center, as well as experts from Macquarie Capital
(Philippines), San Miguel Corporation, AC Energy Holdings, Inc.,
Castillo Laman Tan Pantaleon & San Jose, Herbert Smith Freehills LLP,
ånd Milbank, Tweed, Hadley & McCloy, gave light on the existing
regulatory challenges and the suitable regulatory environment for PPP
capital raising activities. The first roundtable discussion was attended by
local and foreign participants.
On October 9, 2015, a hundred local and foreign participants joined the
second roundtable session entitled "Creating a Suitable Environment for
PPP Project Bonds" held at the ADB. Theassembly focused on issuers'
and investors' general appetite for project bonds as a source of
financing for PPP Projects in the Philippines; the ideal project structure
for PPP bonds; opportunities and challenges for its issuance; the
investment community's criteria in terms of scale, liquidity and
standardization; and the fequisites for a successful project bond offering
in the local market. The resource speakers included representatives
from the SEC, PSE, PPP Center, ADB and the Philippine Dealing and
Exchange Corp., as well as experts from Sumitomo Mitsui Banking
Corporatjon, The Hongkong and Shanghai Banking Corporation
Limited, Credit Guarantee & Investment Facility, Herbert Smith Freehills
LLP, San Miguel Corporation, Standard Chartered Bank, BPI Capital
Corporation, and Thai Bond Market Association (Thailand).
The PSE, PPP Center and SEC will summarize the findings from these
forums and related research and consultations that should lead to action
plans and a framework and regulation to allow PPP firms to access the
capital markets to fund PPP Projects.
The PSE targets to launch its listing rules for PPP firms in the second
half of 2016.
ASEAN Asset Class Marketing Initiative
The ASEAN Exchanges collaboration pursued a number of marketing
and promotional activities in 2015 as part of its ongoing strategy to raise
awareness in the region as a single asset class.
On February 12, the Exchanges participated in Maybank's ASEAN CEO
Summit held in Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia which was highlighted by a
meeting between the ASEAN Exchanges' CEOs and Malaysian Prime
Minister Najib Razak. The meeting underscored the support given by
ASEAN leaders to multiple initiatives launched by the Exchanges, and
also recognized both challenges and opportunities in ASEAN markets
that require continuous cooperation among financial industry regulators
and participants in the region.
Other initiatives for the year included the launch of a revamped ASEAN
Exchanges common website, which now boasts of interesting photos,
facts and figures about ASEAN, social media discussions, analytics
reports on the performance of ASEAN stocks, and a consolidated news
stream of ASEAN markets. Additionally, the ASEAN Exchanges held its
2nd ASEAN Brokers Networking Event in Bali, Indonesia which drew the
participation of more than 100 brokers in the region. The networking
session was aimed at fostering relationships within the ASEAN
brokerage community to generate additional cross-border investment
activity, and facilitate knowledge, information and technology transfers,
among other benefits.

BIGGER MARKET
Hand-Holding Program
The PSE continued its Hand-Holding Program in 2015 to help
companies or entice them to list at the Exchange. The program begins
by acquainting potential listers with the benefits and advantages of
listing, the listing process, and listing requirements. The PSE meets with
the companies several times a year to monitor their progress and further
assist them, if needed. Upon the request of the potential issuer, the PSE
also provides a list of institutions involved in the process of going public
such as the financial advisor, issue manager, underwriter, legal firm,
and external auditor. In 2015, 145 companies from a wide range of
industries in different provinces in the country were assisted by the
Hand-Holding Program of the PSE. Many companies have expressed
interest in listing but have yet to begin any preparations, while some
have taken concrete steps towards going public.
The Hand-Holding Program has played an important part for nearly half
of the companies that went into IPO since 2007.
Philippine Corporate Day in Tokyo, Japan
The PSE and DBP-Daiwa Capital Markets conducted the Daiwa-PSE
Philippine Corporate Day on January 22 to 23, 2015 at the Daiwa
Corporate Offices in Tokyo, Japan to take advantage of the interest of
Japanese investors in the Philippines by highlighting prospects,
performance and opportunities in the country.
The two-day Philippine Corporate Day provided Tokyo-based fund
houses the opportunity to know more about the listed companies which
participated in the event. These include Alliance Global Group, Inc.,
Ayala Corporation, Ayala Land Inc., BDO Unibank Inc., Energy
Development Corporation, JG Summit Holdings Inc., Metro Pacific
Investments Corporation, Petron Corporation, Robinsons Land
Corporation, San Miguel Corporation, Universal Robina Corporation,
and The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc.
The roadshow registered a 37 percent increase in investor turnout and a
58 percent growth in the number of fund houses present from the
previous year. The organizers also saw a 32 percent increase in
meetings between the listed companies and major Japanese investors.
Japanese Listing Forum
The PSE, together with Takara Printing, The International Friendship
Exchange Council, KPMG Japan, KPMG Philippines, and Mori Hamada
& Matsumoto Law Office, held the Philippine Business Environment
Seminar in Tokyo on January 21 , 2015. The seminar provided
executives of 44 Japanese firms with an overview of the condition and
outlook of the Philippine economy and business sector, as well as the
developments in the country's capital market. It served as a venue to
discuss the listing structure and rules of the Exchange to encourage
Japanese firms with Philippine subsidiaries to list at the PSE. The
participants were also given a copy of the PSE's listing rules which was
translated to Japanese by Takara Printing.
Listing 101 for the Philippine Franchise Association
The PSE provided members of the Philippine Franchise Association
(PFA) an overview of the Philippine capital market and the opportunities
available for small-and medium-sized enterprises (SMEs) to expand
their businesses by listing in the PSE. At the PFA'S General Membership
Meeting and Elections held on September 2, 2015 at the Crowne Plaza
Galleria Hotel, the PSE provided some examples of listed SME
companies that raised funds for their strategic projects and eventually
realized increased valuations for their companies.

November 2015 at the DBPIs Bulwagan ng Diwang Pilipino. Attended by
over a hundred participants from small and medium companies, the
forum featured presentations and panel discussions on Initial Public

Products Forum
In 2015, the PSE conducted four PSE Products Forums with the topics
Exchange-Traded Funds (ETFs), Shari'ah-Compliant Securities, Direct
Market Access, and Equity-based Managed Funds.
The PSE Products Forum are designed to inform and update the
investing public on the investment products and services available in tyne
market. Each forum focuses on a specific investment product, which is
discussed by experts in the industry. The forums also serve as a venue
for the exchange of ideas and opinions among members of the investing
community.
The first two forums for the year focused on ETFs. As a relatively new
product in the Philippine market, the forum introduced the ETF to the
investing public — including investment strategy, and how one can take
advantage of the opportunities in this asset class. As most ETFs track
indices, part of the discussion involved market indices.
The third forum featured the Direct Market Access (DMA) service and
Shari'ah program of the PSE.
Meanwhile, one of the forums highlighted fund investment with
presentations on the four most popular equity-based investments in the
country namely, Unit Investment Trust Funds (UITFs), Variable Universal
Life Insurance (VULs), Mutual Funds (MFS), and ETFs. The forum
defined the features of the products, how to invest in them, and what
investors must consider when deciding to invest in these equity-based
investment products.
The PSE Products Forum attracted around 300 participants composed
of capital market practitioners, finance professionals, and retail
investors.
PCCI Partnership
As part of its Memorandum of Agreement with the Philippine Chamber
of Commerce and Industry (PCCI), the PSE participated in three PCCI
Area Business Conferences (ABCs) in 2015. The ABCs, namely Visayas
ABC, South Luzon ABC, and Mindanao ABC, served as a venue for the
PSE to discuss the available investment options and financing
opportunities in the Philippine capital market. The ABCs gathered key
decision makers, local chamber constituents and industry associations
to promote business collaboration and global competitiveness with each
leg being attended by around 200 participants.
The PSE also joined the 41st PCCI Philippine Business Conference and
Exposition (PBC&E), an annual gathering and dialogue of policymakers in
the Executive and Legislative branches of government and of decision
makers in Philippine business. Through this event, the PSE encouraged
participants to invest in the Philippine market. The PSE also distributed

STRONGER GOVERNANCE FRAMEWORK
