Promoting good governance within the
Exchange and among listed companies and
FRAMEWORK
Trading Participants is critical in maintaining
and enhancing the integrity of the stock
market. Equally important is instilling systems
and processes that enhance business
continuity and help minimize systemic risks.
Enterprise Risk Management
Given its vital role in the operation of a stable and efficient capital market, the PSE continued to
make risk management a fundamental part of its strategy development and execution, business
operations and effective corporate governance. The Exchange's Enterprise Risk Management
(ERM) Framework, which was approved by the PSE Board in 2013, formalizes the process of a
comprehensive, systematic and proactive management of risks faced by the Exchange based
on best practices in risk management and governance.
Under its ERM Framework, the PSE identified its Top Key Risks for 2015 as system failure,
compliance, regulations, microeconomics and disasters, among others.
To address these identified risks, the Company has developed various risk mitigation measures
under its business planning process. In 2015, risk monitoring and reporting processes were also
implemented through the development of Key Risk Indicators (KRIs), dashboards and risk
management reports.
•iiiilllt.
48
===== page 53 / 152 =====
Business Continuity Management
In accordance with its Business Continuity Management Policy, the
Exchange conducted Disaster Recovery (DR) tests in 2015: one in May, in
preparation for the implementation of its new trading system, and again in
October, post-implementation of the PSEtrade XTS and with the
participation of more than a hundred Trading Participants (TP), market
vendors and the Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines. The
RSA security tokens were also required to further enhance the security
measures for offsite users accessing both the PSE's primary and DR site.
The tests were conducted to assess the ability of TPs and market
vendors to connect to the PSE's DR site and commence or continue
trading in the event of a system failure or disaster in the Exchange's
primary data center. The DR site is a secure and reliable facility located
in a different topographic location and power grid. It is compliant with
industry standards and best practices in terms of distance from primary
data center, seismic zone and environment site details, accessibility,
capacity for expansion, and network latency. It also has a Tier 3
certification. The power supply inside the facility is fully fault-tolerant and
everything is dual-powered.
Cybersecurity
As technology plays an important role in its operations, the PSE needs
to continuously update its infrastructure to address changing market
trends and adopt new technologies as they are introduced. In 2015, the
Company increased its primary internet bandwidth to cope with the
STRONGER GOVERNANCE FRAMEWORK
higher demand for internet web access and online trading. It also
upgraded the voice gateways and access network switches.
The Exchange Systems Monitoring Facility has also been upgraded to
include two additional LED monitors to display in real-time the Nasdaq
X-Stream servers and firewalls utilizations, events, and summary
dashboards. In this regard, the Exchange implemented the Critical
Alarm Monitoring System (CAMS), which enhances the real-time
notification of alerts among relevant personnel, and installed additional
security devices to counter cyber security attacks. Security appliances
are also installed to detect and prevent vulnerability exploits.
The PSE also upgraded its RSA authentication manager system to
increase security measures for the authorized users as they enter into
the system. Moreover, the Exchange upgraded its Zimbra Mail Server's
firmware to address vulnerability issues.
2015 PSE Bell Awards for Corporate Governance
The PSE Bell Awards for Corporate Governance is an initiative to raise
corporate governance standards in the Philippine market and sustain
the country's image and standing as an investment destination.
Launched in December 2011 , the awards program is intended to
recognize listed companies adhering to the highest standards of
corporate governance in the country and whose corporate governance
practices are at par with international standards. It also recognizes
Trading Participants that have demonstrated excellent compliance with
PSE and SEC rules and regulations and other applicable laws while
contributing to the performance and development of the stock market.

ASEAN Corporate Governance Initiative
The ASEAN Corporate Governance Initiative (CGI) is an initiative of the
ASEAN Capital Markets Fofum and supported by the Asian
Development Bank in preparation for the ASEAN economic integration.
The ASEAN CGI is comprised of (1) the ASEAN Corporate Governance
Scorecard (ACGS), a corporate governance rating system based on the
OECD Prinpiples of Corporate Governance and conducted by
independent peer reviewers in each participating country, and (2) the
corpofate governance ranking of ASEAN publicly listed companies
(PLCs) based on their ACGS scores.
The ASEAN CGI's objectives are to enhance corporate governance
standards among participating ASEAN member countries by
establishing a common governance framework across the region and to
enhance the international visibility and investor appeal of well-governed
ASEAN PLCs and the ASEAN brand as an attractive asset class.
An important milestone of the ASEAN CGI was the conduct of the First
ASEAN Corporate Governance Congress which was held on November
14 in Manila and was hosted by the Philippines' SEC and the ACMF.
This event was the culmination of the 2014-2015 ACGS peer review for
ASEAN PLCs. The event was co-presented by the Asian Development
Bank, in partnership with the PSE, the International Finance
Corporation and the Institute of Corporate Directors (Philippine ACGS
Domestic Ranking Body).
The November 14 Congress consisted of two events:
The winners and finalists were recognized in an awarding ceremony on
November 25, 2015 at the Makati Shangri-La Hotel.

STRONGER GOVERNANCE FRAMEWORK
Continuing Education for Listed Companies' Corporate Information
and Compliance Officers
The PSE conducted its annual lecture series on Listing and Disclosure
Rules on November 9 and 10, 2015. This continuing program aims to
provide listed companies with updates on case studies and best
practices for participants to gain a deeper understanding of the
disclosure requirements of the Exchange.
To enhance the implementation of this lecture series, the Exchange
conducted a survey among listed companies before the seminar to
assess the needs and expectations of the listed companies. The
Exchange aligned the seminar topics based on the results of this survey
which showed need to further train companies on the use of the PSE
EDGE facility. The PSE CGO likewise gave a briefing to the seminar
participants on relevant developments in corporate governance (CG),
particularly the CG Blueprint, CG Scorecard, PSE Bell Awards and the
Revised OECD CG Principles. The Exchange emphasized the
responsibilities of listed companies in complying with the disclosure
requirements and in adopting international best practices and standards
in CG, as an integral part of their obligation to the investing public. The
Exchange stressed as well the guidelines and recommendations in
using the PSE EDGE disclosure templates as these are powerful tools
in fulfilling the listed companies' obligation to provide full, fair, timely and
accurate disclosures. The recent updates and enhancements on the
PSE EDGE system were also discussed in the seminar.
Aside from the annual seminar, the Exchange held one-on-one trainings
for newly listed companies to assist them in complying with the
continuing listing and disclosure requirements of the Exchange.
Delisting
Part of PSE's function as a Self-Regulatory Organization is to ensure that
the roster of listed firms is composed of companies that comply with the
rules and regulations of the Exchange. In enforcing the rules governing
listed companies, the Exchange initiated involuntary delisting proceedings
on Marsteel Consolidated, Inc. and Gotesco Land, Inc. due to the
continuing violation of PSE rules. The shares of Marsteel Consolidated,
Inc. were delisted from the Official Registry of the Exchange effective
August 15, 2015, while Gotesco Land, Inc. was delisted on March 18,
2016.
Trading Participants' Rules
In line with ensuring the effective and proper enforcement of the PSE
Rules Governing Trading Rights and Trading Participants (TP Rules),
the Exchange continued its documentation review of Trading
Participant's records to assess and address compliance issues and
strengthen TPs' compliance with the Rules. The efforts on compliance
management contributed to the significant improvement in the
submission of mandatory compliance reports by Trading Participants for
the year 2015. The Exchange also drafted proposed amendments to the
TP Rules to give more guidance on compliance to certain provisions
and provide for new procedures and policy developments that will fill
gaps in implementation and define mandates in line with the CMIC
Rules. The proposed amendments to the TP Rules are targeted to be
finalized in 2016.
The Exchange also continued to introduce process improvements to
make compliance monitoring and record keeping more efficient.

REPORT
The PSE acknowledges the need to address
extra-financial themes to support the call for a
sustainable global economy.
As part of our journey to define our medium- and
long-term sustainability efforts, we take a holistic
look at the impact of our regular activities. We are
taking stock to enhance and transform them to
support social and environmental development
within the context of the bigger community. All our
stakeholders are affected by our actions, so it is
important to take their feedback to serve as our
guide in both policy-making and implementation.
Given our role as a regulator, being a good
corporate citizen has to be at the core of the values
of the PSE. I am proud to say that our management
team and employees live out this mission as
reflected in our corporate values and our Code of
Ethics and Business Conduct. This is our response
to the inherent duty to run the Exchange in an
orderly and efficient manner and make our business
more responsible and responsive.
We are committed advocates of sustainability and
the reporting of such efforts to our shareholders.
Thank you.
Hans B. Sicat

SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
SUSTAINABLE BUSINESS
Corporate Governance is at the forefront of our Sustainable
Business initiatives. Our ultimate goal is to expand awareness
among listed companies and trading participants about excellent
CG practices. We realize at the Exchange that to be effective
advocates, we must exhibit these by example.
Disclosure
The PSE champions high standard of disclosure and transparency.
To help investors make informed decisions, the Company ensures
timely and accurate disclosures of all material information. The
PSE also distributes pertinent information about the company in its
annual reports and press releases. The disclosures can be
accessed at the PSE Electronic Disclosure Generation Technology
or PSE EDGE through http://edge.pse.com.ph/ while all press
releases are found in the PSE website.
Information for Stakeholders
The PSE organizes its annual stockholders' meeting to give
shareholders update on the Company's performance and an
opportunity to dialogue with PSE officials. During the annual
meeting, the PSE provides stockholders its annual report.
To keep both internal and external stakeholders well-informed of
company news, the PSE sends its bi-monthly corporate
newsletter called the PSE Bulletin.
The Exchange also utilizes its Investor Relations webs*lte
http://www.pse.com.ph/investorRelations/home.html, for
shareholders to access company information such as financial
reports, company disclosures, dividend history, stock price,
company presentations, shareholder information, press releases,
and the newsletter. The PSE also shares information with
stakeholders through regular postings of stock market news and
alerts in its social media platforms, i.e., Facebook and Twitter.
Conflict of Interest
The PSE has an obligation to the marketplace and to its
shareholders to make sound business decisions that are not
influenced by the personal interests of its employees. This means
that PSE employees are expected to act in the interest of the
Exchange, without favor or preference based on possible direct or
indirect personal gain. Employees must avoid and, if needed,
remove themselves from relationships and activities that may lead
to a conflict of interest or the appearance of a conflict of interest.
Corporate Governance Programs
On November 25, 2015, the PSE held the 4th PSE Bell Awards
for Corporate Governance to honor listed companies and trading
participants that practice the highest standards of corporate
governance in the country. The Bell Awards is an initiative to raise
corporate governance standards in the Philippine market and
sustain the country's image and standing as an investment
destination in the country and whose corporate governance
practices are at par with international standards.
The PSE, in partnership with the SEC and with technical
assistance and funding from the USAID and the Makati Business
Club/lntegrity for Investments Initiative, also held a Corporate
Governance Forum on October 15, 2015 that aimed to discuss
the Implementing Rules and Regulations of the Securities
Regulation Code featuring panel discussions with selected listed
companies and institutional investors.
The Exchange was likewise actively involved in the development
of the Philippine Corporate Governance Blueprint, an initiative of
the SEC to develop and launch a new and comprehensive
market-wide corporate governance framework and strategy to
54
raise corporate governance standards in the Philippine capital
markets within the next five years. The Blueprint, which was
launched during the ASEAN Capital Markets Forum's ASEAN
Corporate Governance Congress on November 14, was designed
to guide corporate governance rule development and
implementation and raise Philippine corporate governance
standards through public and private enforcement. The forum also
served as basis for enhancing the 2009 Revised Code of
Corporate Governance and other rules and regulations that can
be used to encourage or enforce the adoption of corporate
governance best practices.
During the Blueprint's development process, the PSE fook an
active role in the discussions and was the lead institution in two of
the sub-committees formed to address different corporate
governance topics based on the OECD Prihciples of Corporate
Governance, i.e. the role of institutional investors and the
enforcement of corporate governance rules.
The PSE also took part in the ASEAN Corporate Governance
Initiative (CGI). Organized by the ASEAN Capital Markets Forum
(ACMF) with the sup$ort of the Asian Development Bank in
preparation for the ASEAN economic integration, the initiative
aims to enhance corporate governance standards among
participating ASEAN member countries by establishing a
common governance framework across the region and to
enhance the international visibility and investor appeal of
åvell-governed ASEAN publicly listed companies (PLCs) and the
ASEAN brand internationally as an attractive asset class. A key
component to this initiative is the ASEAN Corporate Governance
Scorecard (ACGS), a corporate governance rating system based
on the OECD Principles of Corporate Governance and conducted
by independent peer reviewers in each participating country. The
other activity is corporate governance ranking of ASEAN PLCs
based on their ACGS scores.
As a milestone of the ASEAN CGI and the culmination of the
2014-2015 ACGS peer reviews of ASEAN PLCs, the First ASEAN
Corporate Governance Congress was held on November 14 in
Manila and was hosted by the Philippines' SEC and the ACMF,
with the PSE as a partner in the event. The Congress consisted of
a half-day conference with the theme "Governance
Transformation in ASEAN: Reform and Priorities',' held at the
Grand Ballroom of the Asian Development Bank. Together with
this, the ASEAN Corporate Governance Awards Night was held at
the Manila Polo Club where the Top 50 ASEAN Publicly Listed
Companies, the Top 5 ASEAN PLCs, and the Top 3 and two most
improved PLCs per country were all recognized. Both events were
attended by ACMF officials, heads of ASEAN publicly listed
companies and various corporate governance advocates from
across the region.
Of the six participating ASEAN markets, the Philippines had the
second highest number of PLCs in the Top 50 ASEAN list with 11
companies. These outstanding companies are Philex Mining
Corporation, Globe Telecom, Inc., Ayala Land, Inc., Ayala
Corporation, Philippine Long Distance Telephone Company, SM
Prime Holdings, Inc., Manila Electric Company, GT Capital
Holdings, Inc., BDO Unibank, Inc., Manila Water Company, Inc.,
and Aboitiz Equity Ventures.
Business Continuity
As risk management and business continuity are vital in
generating long-term value for shareholders, the PSE realizes the
need to enhance its capability to continuously maintain business
functions under stressful environments and to prepare for
unexpected interruptions to its operations.
The PSE continues to improve its resilience and sustainability by
identifying and addressing potential business risks. Under its
Enterprise Risk Management (ERM) Framework, the PSE
identified its top key risks and the corresponding risk mitigation
measures as part of its business planning process. Risk
monitoring and reporting processes were also implemented
===== page 59 / 152 =====
throughÄhe development of Key Risk Indicators, dashboards and
risk manggement reports.
In 2015, the Exchange also continued to support the operational
Öesiliency of market participants. It conducted several Disaster
Recovery (DR) tests to assess the ability of trading participants
and market vendors to connect to the PSE's DR site and
commence or continue trading in the event of a system failure or
disaster in the Exchange's primary data center. The DR site, a
secure and reliable facility located in a different topographic
location and power grid, is compliant with industry standards and
best practices in terms of distance from primary data center,
seismic zone and environment site details, accessibility, capacity
for expansion, and network latency. It also has a Tier 3
certification, and its power supply is fully fault-tolerant and
dual-powered.
Seminar for Listed Companies
The PSE held a lecture series on the Listing and Disclosure
Rules on November 9 and 10, 2015. The two-day seminar
provided participants with a deeper understanding of listed
companies' responsibilities in complying with the disclosure
requirements and in adopting international best practices and
standards in corporate governance. The Exchange also
emphasized in the lecture series certain guidelines and
recommendations in using the PSE EDGE system. The
participants were also given a briefing on relevant developments
in corporate governance.
To assist the newly listed companies in complying with the listing
and disclosure requirements, the Exchange provided one-on-one
trainings on using the PSE EDGE and implementing good
disclosure practices.
Trainings for Brokers for the New Trading System
To ensure the smooth transition to the PSEtrade XTS trading
platform, the PSE launched activities to address the system
change through training and certification for traders, rehearsals
for all market participants, and a series of broker readiness
forums in both Ayala and Tektite offices.

Employee Engagement
Crucial in achieving the Exchange's goals is an engaged workforce
committed towards a more developed capital market. To ensure
employees meet the highest standards of excellence in disposing
their functions, the PSE endeavors to keep a highly motivated and
capable workforce.
Learning and Development
To help employees improve in their current roles, the PSE
conducted the following in-house training programs:
Effective Presentation Skills Program that empowered
employees to create easy-to-understand and commanding
presentations
Project Management Fundamentals that helped employees
develop a methodical approach to achieving project
objectives; and,
Basic Supervisory Skills Training that provided supervisors
as well as managers with skills needed to effectively lead
and handle a work group.
The PSE, likewise, held a briefing on the Code of Ethics and
Business Conduct and Social Media Policy to ensure that
employees become aware of how they should behave in and out
the office. The briefing relayed to employees legal and ethical
standards mindful that their action can directly or indirectly affect
the image of the company.
The Exchange also gave an orientation on Information Security to
educate employees on how to prevent data and other company
assets from being compromised, hacked or lost. Moreover, the
PSE conducted a briefing on the PSE Helpdesk System, an
enterprise-wide system that allows online submission of service
requests and incident reports.
To intensify its learning and development programs for
employees, the PSE touched base with the Chartered Institute for
Securities and Investment (CISI), a London-based professional
body offering qualifications, training and industry membership for

offered complimentary CISI membership and Fundamentals of
Financial Services (FFS) examination to members of PSE's

===== page 63 / 152 =====
Best Stock Exchange Award
The PSE takes pride in being named as the Best Stock Exchange in
Southeast Asia in 2015 by institutional investment magazine Alpha
Southeast Asia in its Marquee Awards held during the 9th Alpha
Southeast Asia Deal & Solution Awards in Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia.
The Marquee Awards surveyed issuers and investors in the Southeast
Asian region to determine the winner in its various award categories.
As Best Stock Exchange, the PSE was recognized for its initiatives to
have world-class standards for disclosures and corporate governance. It
was also cited for the shift to the PSEtrade XTS trading engine which
was accomplished in eleven months, much shorter than the two years it
took during the last adoption of a new trading system. The award-giving
body also noted the stellar climb of the PSE index (PSEi) to the 8,000
level in April 2015 as well as the capital raising activity in the Exchange.
This is the second time in two years that the PSE received this
recognition from Alpha Southeast Asia magazine. In 2013, the PSE was
also conferred the Best Stock Exchange in Southeast Asia award.
Stock Exchange Innovation Award
As the PSE works toward realizing its vision of being a premiere
exchange with word-class standards, an organization of former
Exchange Executives took note of the accomplishments by the PSE to
MILESTONE RECOGNITIONS
reach this goal. The International Stock Exchange Executives Emeriti
(ISEEE) presented the first ISEEE Lennar International Award for Stock
Exchange Innovation to the PSE.
The Stock Exchange Innovation Award was given in recognition for
innovation in the areas of governance, transparency and information
quality, integrity, efficiency, liquidity, transaction costs, and technology in
capital markets around the world.
The awarding ceremony was held during the Florida Global Market
Innovation Conference in March and was attended by former stock
exchange executives from over 20 countries. PSE President and CEO
Hans B. Sicat joined the event through a video conference facility. Aside
from receiving the awards trophy, Mr. Sicat was also interviewed by
Lennar International President Chris Marlin about the developments on
the Philippine economy and the equities market.
Anvil Award
The PSE won a Gold Anvil Award for its 2014 annual report entitled
"Plans in Motion'.' The award was received by representatives of the
Exchange during the 51st Anvil Awards Ceremony held on February 26,
2016 at the Makati Shangri-La Hotel.
The Anvil Award is given by the Public Relations Society of the
Philippines to outstanding public relations programs and tools that meet
the highest standards set in its various categories.

REPORT OF SUBSIDIARIES
CAPITAL MARKETS
INTEGRITY CORPORATION
The Capital Markets Integrity Corporation (CMIC) is the independent
audit, surveillance and compliance unit of the PSE principally
responsible for carrying out the enforcement role of a self-regulatory
organization (SRO) in pursuance of the securities laws. The CMIC has
the jurisdiction to investigate and resolve violations of the securities
laws, including trading-related irregularities and unusual trading
activities.
The CMIC is composed of three core departments: the Surveillance
Department (SD); the Audit and Compliance Department (ACD); and the
Investigation and Enforcement Department (IED). These three
departments contribute in fulfilling the CMIC's role of safeguarding the
interests of the investing public and maintaining the integrity of the
capital markets.
Surveillance
The SD's primary functions are monitoring of daily market activities,
identification of irregularities in the market, and evaluation of unfair
trading practices. The SD initially evaluated a total of 66 cases, 20 of
which were referred to the IED for investigation. In October, SD
commenced the implementation of the electronic submission of required
documents by the concerned parties via the EDGE Submission System
of the PSE to enhance its monitoring capabilities.
Audit and Compliance
The ACD conducts a regular audit of all Trading Participants (T PS) and,
based on defined parameters, a spot audit of certain T Ps. The ACD,
through its Regular Examination Unit (REU), completed its regular audit
of all one hundred thirty-two TPs. As of end 2015, 80 percent of the TPs
were found to have had no material violations, an improvement from the
76 percent figure in 2014. Meanwhile, the ACD's Risk Management Unit
(RMU) conducted spot audits of 41 T PS, and concluded that 93 percent
had no material violations. The RMU is in charge of determining the TPs'
compliance with the risk based capital adequacy ratio (RBCA) and the
reserve requirement, among other requisites provided by the securities
laws.
Investigation and Enforcement
The IED conducts investigations to determine possible violations
against, and enforces compliance with, pertinent securities laws. In
2015, the IED received and consequently investigated cases filed by
investors and twenty cases endorsed by the SD, involving purported
trading-related irregularities and unusual trading activities, among other
violations of the securities laws. Six of the SD-endorsed cases were, in
turn, forwarded by the IED to the Securities and Exchange Commission
(SEC) for further investigation and/or appropriate action.
Takeover of Operations
In February, the CMIC filed with the SEC a petition to take over the
operations of a TP, after duly ascertaining that there were sufficient
grounds to support this action. Subsequently, in May, the SEC issued an
Order granting the CMIC's petition for the issuance of a takeover order
against the TP, pursuant to the pertinent securities laws, which the
CMIC complied with.
Voluntary Suspension
In March, the CMIC approved the application for voluntary suspension filed
by a TP.
Seminars and Trainings
The CMIC organized seminars for TPs on February 9, 11, and 13 pertaining
64
to best audit practices and review of risk-based capital adequacy; salient
provisions of the CMIC Rules and other securities laws; and the Foreign
Account Tax Compliance Act or FATCA. The seminars were attended by
associated persons, compliance officers, accountants, and personnel from
several TPs and other market participants.
In addition, the CMIC continued to provide training for its staff to equip them
with the proficiencies essential to adhering to the corporation's fundamental
standards and values of professionalism, integrity, teamwork, competence,
honesty, and fairness.
For 2015, the CMIC participated irÜhe following seminars:
•
•
•
Regional Seminar on Enforcement including Cross Border under
the Asia-Pacific; Economic Cooperation Financial Regulators
Training Initiative in Shanghai, People's Republic of China;
RegioynafTraining Program on Effective Oversight of Capital
Markets held in Taipei, Taiwan;
Briefing and consultation on the Foreign Accounts Tax Compliance
Act or FATCA in Manila;
Continuing training to the Total Market Surveillance, in Seoul,
South Korea;
19th ASEAN Federation of Accountants Conference held in Makati
City; and
Automatic Exchange of Information in Manila.
Amendment of Rules
In view of the developments in capital market laws, the CMIC has
revisited and drafted revisions to its rules consistent with creating a
fundamentally objective, integrally dynamic, and essentially progressive
capital market industry.

GENERAL NATURE AND SCOPE OF BUSINESS OF REGISTRANT AND ITS SUBSIDIARIES
The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. was incorporated on July 14, 1992 as a non-stock
corporation and later became a stock corporation on August 3, 2001. On December 15, 2003,
pursuant to the demutualization mandate of Republic Act No. 8799, or the Securities Regulation
Code, the Company's outstanding capital stock was listed by way of introduction. On February 18,
2004, the Company sold 6,077,505 shares from its unissued stock to five strategic investors by
way of private placement that was approved by the Securities and Exchange Commission. The
strategic investors were the PLDT Beneficial Trust Fund, SMC Retirement Fund, Government
Service Insurance System, Kim Eng Investment, Ltd., and KE Strategic Pte. Ltd.
The Company's revenues are primarily derived from listing-related fees. The Company
charges listing fees for initial public offerings and additional listings, and for annual listing
maintenance. Other sources of revenue are membership, transaction, data feed, and
miscellaneous fees, which include service fees. Membership and transaction fees are charged to
trading participants while data feed fees are collected from data vendors.
Subsidiary and Affiliates
The Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines (SCCP) is a wholly-owned
subsidiary organized primarily as a clearance, settlement and depository agency for SCCP-eligible
trades executed through the facilities of the Company. SCCP is responsible for (a) synchronizing
the settlement of funds for transactions of clearing members and the transfer of securities through
Delivery versus Payment (DVP) clearing; (b) the administration of the Clearing and Trade Guaranty
Fund (CTGF) and guaranteeing the settlement of trade through the implementation of its Fails
Management System in the event of a Trading Participant's default; and (c) performance of Risk
Management and Monitoring to ensure final and irrevocable settlement. Recently, the Securities
and Exchange Commission provisionally granted SCCP the license to operate a depository.
The Capital Markets Integrity Corporation (CMIC) is also a wholly-owned subsidiary. It
functions as the independent audit, surveillance and compliance unit of the Company, having taken
over the Market Regulation Division of the Company. CMIC operates to ensure the integrity of the
capital market and has jurisdiction to investigate all violations of the Securities Regulation Code
and CMIC Rules by Trading Participants, and investigate trading-related irregularities and unusual
trading activities committed by Issuers. The Securities and Exchange Commission granted CMIC
its authority to operate as the independent audit, surveillance and compliance unit of the Company
with provisional self-regulatory organization status on 2 February 2012.
The Company is also a shareholder of the Philippine Dealing System Holdings Corporation
(PDSHC), the holding company of the Philippine Dealing & Exchange Corporation (PDEX),
otherwise known as the Fixed Income Exchange (FIE), the Philippine Depository & Trust Corp.
(PDTC) and the Philippines Securities Settlement Corporation (PSSC). As of 29 February 2016,
the Company is a stockholder of record of 1,311 ,439 shares, or 20.98%, of all of the issued and
outstanding shares.
Properties
The Company is the registered owner of offices at the PSE Centre in Ortigas Center, Pasig
City, and the PSE Plaza along Ayala Avenue, Makati City.
In addition, over a seven-year period beginning January 2005, the outstanding shares of
Crescent West Development Corporation (CWDC), a subsidiary of Fort Bonifacio Development
Corporation (FBDC) and the registered owner of a 2,182 square meter lot in Bonifacio Global City,
will be transferred to the Company. In June 2007, the donation of all remaining CWDC shares was
deferred pending negotiations among the Company, FBDC and Ayala Land, Inc. (ALI) for the joint
development of an office building in Bonifacio Global City to house the Company's headquarters,
management offices and unified operations in equities and securities for the National Capital
===== page 83 / 152 =====
Region. The Company provisionally approved the relocation of its headquarters and offices to a
new building located at Bonifacio Global City to be developed by ALI.
In May 2015, FBDC and the Company signed a Contract to Sell for the purchase by PSE of
office units in Bonifacio Global City to house the Company's unified offices at a total purchase price
of Php805,369,600. Simultaneously with the signing of the Contract to Sell, PSE sold back the
donated CWDC shares to FBDC. The proceeds of the sale of CWDC shares received by the
Company and the remaining portion of the agreed accommodation value of the 2,182 square meter
lot will be applied against the total purchase price. The turnover of the office units, is expected to
happen in 2016. The move-in is programmed by 2017.

dividend to stockholders of record as of 25 March 2015 payable on 16 April 2015.
Dividend Policy
The Company adopts a policy for the declaration of regular cash dividend out of the
unrestricted retained earnings equivalent to fifty percent (50%) of the Company's audited net
income. The declaration of dividends is dependent on the cash flow, business plan and financial
condition of the Company.
Recent Sale of Unregistered/Exempt Securities
The common shares granted to eligible employees pursuant to the Employee Stock
Purchase Plan is an exempt transaction under Section 10.2 of the Securities Regulation Code, as
stated under Resolution No. 179 Series of 2008 of the Securities and Exchange Commission.
At the annual meeting of the stockholders of the Company held on 2 May 2015, the
Stockholders approved by a vote representing at least 2/3 of the outstanding capital stock the
ESPP (pursuant to Resolution No. 41 , Series of 2015 of the Board of Directors of the Corporation)
with the following terms and conditions:
Number of shares to be offered will be 150,000 shares or about 0.25% of the
outstanding capital stock of the Exchange as of December 31, 2014. Each offering
shall consist of 50,000 shares.
The Program shall be administered by a five-member ESPP Committee headed by
the President and CEO of the Exchange and the Corporate Governance &
Compliance Officer and Vice President of the Exchange, Chief Operating Officer of
the Exchange, General Counsel of the Exchange, and the Chief Operating Officer of
SCCP. The secretariat of the ESPP Committee will be led by the Head of the
Human Resources & Administration Department of the Exchange.
All regular employees, who are not under any suspension of the Exchange, and
SCCP with at least one year of service shall be eligible for the ESPP.
Offer date shall be quarterly for a period of three (3) full years exercisable within
January to December of each year commencing in 2015. Any un-availed portion of
the ESPP shares offered to the employees within any year shall be reallocated to
the immediately succeeding year.
Offer price shall be fixed based on the volume weighted Average Price (VWAP) of
PSE shares for the month preceding the offer date less a discount of ten percent
(10%). Payment terms will be through monthly salary deduction over a period of
twenty four (24) months subject to payroll guidelines on Net take home pay.
Mandatory holding period of two (2) years from date of subscription shall be
implemented.
If an employee who availed of the ESPP shares resigns within the mandatory
holding period, then the Exchange will reimburse the resigned employee for any and
all contributions paid as of the latest payroll cutoff.
In case an employee dies pending completion of the full payment over the 24-month
period, any remaining unpaid balance will be considered by the Exchange as fully-
paid.

2015 REPORT OF THE AUDIT COMMITTEE
The Audit Committee is the body tasked with assisting the Board of Directors of the Exchange in the
performance of its supervision and control over the financial reporting process, system Of internal controls,
audit process, and monitoring of compliance with applicable laws, rules and regulations. The Audit
Committee directly exercises oversight authority over the financia I management functions of the
Exchange, as well as oversight and direct interface with its internal and external auditors, It is composed of
six members and chaired by an independent director.
In accordance with its mandate, the Committee confirms that:
It held a total of 11 meetings during the year, in which a majority of the members were present;
• It discussed and approved the scope, frequency and risk-based audit plan of the Internal Audit Group of
the Exchange, covering the following key risk areas as identified and assessed: I) Business Process
Review-Processing of Listing Applications andlmposition of Penalties, 2) Financial Review-
Controllership and Treasury 3) Regulatory Compliance 4) Business Process Review-Trading
Participants Regulations 5) Incident Management Review and 6)) Project Management Review ;
• It reviewed the Internal Audit Group's reports and recommendations, and ensured communication to
Management of their findings and recommendations for timely and corrective action;
• It reviewed the Internal Control System ofthe Exchange covering the year 2015 and was evaluated as
effective and adequate;
• It recommended to the Board Of Directors the appointment of SGV &co, as the independent external
auditor of the Exchange for the year 2015, after an evaluation of their qualification and past
performance;
• It reviewed and approved the audit scope and approach of SGV &COT , assessed their performance in
relation to their engagement, and approved all audit services rendered for 2015, including the related
fees for such services after concluding that such services do not impair their independence;
• It reviewed and discussed with management and SGV &Co, the annual consolidated financial statements
of the Exchange, and its subsidiaries, the Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines and Capital
Markets Integrity Corporation as of December 31, 2015, before submission to the Board;
e It reviewed and discussed with Management the proposed 2016 Budget and endorsed its approval to the
Board with some revisions; and
• It continued to exercise oversight over the risk management function of the Exchange, including the
identification and assessment of the Top Key Risks, the development of business unit business
continuity plans, the holding of several disaster preparedness drillsand system disaster recovery tests
until a separate Risk Management Committee was created by the Board on September 23, 2015 to
handle the said function.
The Committee performed this function in the following context:
Management retains primary responsibility for the financial statements and for assessing the
effectiveness of internal control over financial reporting; and
===== page 87 / 152 =====
• SGV &Co. is responsible for expressing an opinion on the conformity of the annual consolidated financial
statements with Philippine Financial Reporting Standards.
In keeping with the mandate of the Committee and based on the reviews and discussions undertaken, the
Committee recommended to the Board of Directors of the Exchange —
the inclusion of its audited consolidated fingncial statements as of December 31, 2015 in the
Annual Report to the Stockholders; and
the filing of the audited consolidated financial statements with the Securities and Exchange

STATEMENT OF MANAGEMENT'S RESPONSIBILITY FOR FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
The management of The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. and Subsidiaries is responsible for
the preparation and fair presentation of the consolidated financial statements which comprise the
consolidated balance sheets as of December 31, 2015 and 2014, and the consolidated statements of
comprehensive income, statements of changes in equity and statements of cash flows for each of the
three years in the period ended December 31, 2015, including the additional components attached
therein, in accordance with the Philippine Fnancial Reporting Standards, This responsibility includes
designing and implementing internal controls relevant to the preparation and fair presentation of
financial statements that are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, selecting
and applying appropriate accounting policies, and making accounting estimates that are reasonable in
the circumstances.
The Board of Directors reviews and approves the financial statements and submits the same to
the stockholders.
SyCip, Gorres, Velayo & Co. (SGV & Co.)l the independent auditors, appointed by the
stockholders, has examined the consolidated financial statements of The Philippine Stook Exchange,
Inc. and Subsidiaries in accordance with Phjlippine Standards on Auditing, and its report to the Board
of Directors and stockholders, has expressed its opinion on the fairness of presentation upon
completion of such examination.

INDEPENDENT AUDITORS' REPORT
The Stockholders and the Board of Directors
The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc.
We have audited the accompanying consolidated financial statements of The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. and
Subsidiaries, which comprise the consolidated statements of financial position as at December 31 , 2015 and 2014, and
the consolidated statements of comprehensive income, statements of changes in equity and statements of cash flows for
the years ended December 31, 2015 and 2014, and a summary of significant accounting policies and other explanatory
information.
Management's Responsibility for the Consolidated Financial Statements
Management is responsible for the preparation and fair presentation of these consolidated financial statements in
accordance with Philippine Financial Reporting Standards, and for such internal control as management determines is
necessary to enable the preparation of consolidated financial statements that are free from material misstatement,
whether due to fraud or error.
Auditors' Responsibility
Our responsibility is to express an opinion on these consolidated financial statements based on our audits. We
conducted our audits in accordance with Philippine Standards on Auditing. Those standards require that we comply with
ethical requirements and plan and perform the audit to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated
financial statements are free from material misstatement.
An audit involves performing procedures to obtain audit evidence about the amounts and disclosures in the consolidated
financial statements. The procedures selected depend on the auditor's judgment, including the assessment of the risks
of material misstatement of the consolidated financial statements, whether due to fraud or error. In making those risk
assessments, the auditor considers internal control relevant to the entity's preparation and fair presentation of the
consolidated financial statements in order to design audit procedures that are appropriate in the circumstances, but not
for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the entity's internal control. An audit also includes
evaluating the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates made by
management, as well as evaluating the overall presentation of the consolidated financial statements.
We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our audit
opinion.
Opinion
In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements present fairly, in all material respects, the financial position of The
Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. and Subsidiaries as at December 31 , 2015 and 2014, and their financial performance
and their cash flows for each of the years ended December 31 , 2015 and 2014 in accordance with Philippine Financial
Reporting Standards.
Other Matter
The consolidated financial statements of The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. and Subsidiaries as at and for the year
ended December 31, 2013 were audited by another auditor who expressed an unqualified opinion on those statements
on March 12, 2014.

THE PHILIPPINE STOCK EXCHANGE, INC. AND SUBSIDIARIES
NOTES TO CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
Corporate Information
1.
The Philippine Stock Exchange, Inc. (the Parent Company or the Exchange) was incorporated in
the Philippines on July 14, 1992 as a non-stock corporation primarily to provide and maintain a
convenient and suitable market for the exchange, purchase and sale of all types of securities and
other instruments.
On August 8, 2001 , the Parent Company was converted from a non-stock corporation to a stock
corporation (demutualization) with an authorized capital stock of P36.8 million divided into
36.8 million shares with a par value of Pl .00 per share as prescribed by Republic Act (RA)
No. 8799 entitled "Securities Regulation Code" (SRC) and pursuant to a conversion plan approved
by the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC).
The salient features of the demutualization plan approved by the SEC on August 3, 2001 include,
among others, the following:
a.
b.
c.
d.
e.
Conversion of the Parent Company into a stock corporation by amending its Articles of
Incorporation and by-laws;
Subscription of each member of 50,000 shares at Pl .00 per share. The remaining balance of
the Membership Contributions account of P277.4 million shall be treated as additional paid-in
capital;
Issuance of trading rights to brokers in recognition of the existing seat ownership by the
brokers;
Separation of ownership of shares and right to operate as a trading participant in the
Exchange. The trading rights shall be transferable without time limitation; and
Imposition of a moratorium on the issuance of the new trading rights.
On December 15, 2003, the Parent Company's shares of stock were listed by way of introduction
of its outstanding shares to comply with the requirements mandated by the SRC, particularly the
conversion of the Parent Company into a stock corporation.
On January 28, 2004, the Parent Company offered 6,077,505 unissued shares to the private
sector as part of its on-going effort to comply with SRC's mandate regarding the ownership of the
Exchange (see Note 18). Gross proceeds from the private placement offering amounted to
P726.3 million, inclusive of additional paid-in capital of P720.2 million representing premium over
the par value of the common stock. Expenses related to the offering amounting to P21.1 million
were recorded as a reduction of the additional paid-in capital.
Securities Clearing Corporation of the Philippines (SCCP), a 100% owned subsidiary of the
Exchange, is a domestic corporation organized to carry out and strictly implement the following
functions: (1 ) Delivery-versus-Payment trade settlement; (2) fails management and administration
of the Clearing and Trade Guaranty Fund (CTGF); and (3) risk monitoring and management.
To ensure compliance of trading participants, SCCP is authorized by the SEC to impose fines and
penalties and other sanctions as approved by SCCP's Board of Directors (BOD).
SCCP was given a temporary license to operate by the SEC and started its commercial operations
on January 3, 2000. On January 15, 2002, the SEC approved SCCP's request for a permanent
license as a clearing agency subject to its compliance with the requirements of Section 42 of the
SRC entitled "Registration of Clearing Agency".
===== page 97 / 152 =====
2.
Capital Markets Integrity Corporation (CMIC), a 100% - owned subsidiary of the Exchange, is a
stock corporation organized on March 14, 2011 to function as the independent audit, surveillance
and compliance unit of the Exchange with the authority to adopt, enforce, implement and interpret
rules, guidelines and securities laws applicable to the operations and dealings of trading
participants and other market participants of the Exchange.
The registered office address of the Parent Company is 2nd Floor, PSE Plaza, Ayala Triangle,
Ayala Avenue, Makati City.
The accompanying consolidated financial statements were authorized for issue by the BOD on
February 24, 2016.
Basis of Preparation and Consolidation and Changes in Accounting Policies and
Disclosures
Basis of Preparation
The consolidated financial statements of the Parent Company and its subsidiaries (collectively
referred to as the Group) have been prepared in compliance with Philippine Financial Reporting
Standards (PFRS).
The Group's consolidated financial statements have been prepared on a historical cost basis,
except for financial assets at fair value through profit or loss (FVPL) and available-for-sale (AFS)
investments, which are measured at fair value. The consolidated financial statements are
presented in Philippine peso, which is the Parent Company's functional and presentation currency
under PFRS. All values are rounded to the nearest peso, except when otherwise indicated.
The consolidated financial statements provide comparative information in respect of the previous
period.
Basis of Consolidation
The consolidated financial statements comprise the financial statements of the Parent Company
and its subsidiaries as at December 31 , 2015 and 2014 and for each of the three years in the
period ended December 31 , 2015. The financial statements of the Subsidiaries are prepared for
the same reporting year as the Parent Company, using consistent accounting policies.
Control is achieved when the Group is exposed, or has rights, to variable returns from its
involvement with the investee and has the ability to affect those returns through its power over the
investee. Specifically, the Group controls an investee if and only if the Group has:
Power over the investee (i.e., existing rights that give it the current ability to direct the relevant
activities of the investee);
Exposure, or rights, to variable returns from its involvement with the investee; and
The ability to use its power over the investee to affect its returns.
Generally, there is a presumption that a majority of voting rights result in control. When the Group
has less than a majority of the voting or similar rights of an investee, the Group considers all
relevant facts and circumstances in assessing whether it has power over an investee, including:
The contractual arrangement with the other vote holders of the investee;
Rights arising from other contractual arrangements; or
The Group's voting rights and potential voting rights.
The Group re-assesses whether or not it controls an investee if facts and circumstances indicate
that there are changes to one or more of the three elements of control. Consolidation of a
subsidiary begins when the Group obtains control over the subsidiary and ceases when the Group
loses control of the subsidiary. Assets, liabilities, income and expenses of a subsidiary acquired
or disposed of during the year are included in the consolidated financial statements from the date
the Group gains control until the date the Group ceases to control the subsidiary.
===== page 98 / 152 =====
Profit or loss and each component of other comprehensive income (OCI) are attributed to the
equity holders of the Parent Company and to the non-controlling interests, even if this results in
the non-controlling interests having a deficit balance. When necessary, adjustments are made to
the financial statements of subsidiaries to bring their accounting policies into line with the Group's
accounting policies. All intra-group assets and liabilities, equity, income, expenses and cash flows
relating to transactions between members of the Group are eliminated in full on consolidation.
A change in the ownership interest of a subsidiary, without a loss of control, is accounted for as an
equity transaction. If the Group loses control over a subsidiary, it:
Derecognizes the assets (including goodwill) and liabilities of the subsidiary;
Derecognizes the carrying amount of any non-controlling interests;
Derecognizes the cumulative translation differences recorded in equity;
Recognizes the fair value of the consideration received;
Recognizes the fair value of any investment retained;
Recognizes any gain or loss in profit or loss; and
Reclassifies the parent's share of components previously recognized in OCI to profit or loss or
retained earnings, as appropriate, as would be required if the Group had directly disposed of
the related assets or liabilities.
Non-controlling interests represent the interests in the subsidiaries not held by the Parent
Company, and are presented separately in the consolidated statements of comprehensive income
and within equity in the consolidated statements of financial position, separately from equity
attributable to holders of the Parent Company.
The consolidated financial statements include the accounts of the Parent Company and the
following wholly-owned subsidiaries as at December 31 , 2015 and 2014:
